1	Dell Latitude 7450	Each	EAN13	8801643123456
2	Dell Latitude 7450	Carton (5 pcs)	Code128	CTN-DL7450-05
3	Bashundhara OPC Cement 50 KG	Each (Bag)	EAN13	8801643789012
7.3.2 Table: ItemRFID
Purpose: RFID tag assignment, typically at the individual-unit level for high-value or asset-tracked items rather than at the packaging level.
Primary Key: ItemRFIDId (BIGINT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: ItemId → ItemMaster; ItemSerialId → ItemSerial (nullable)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ItemRFIDId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ItemId	BIGINT	No	—	Item this tag identifies
ItemSerialId	BIGINT	Yes	NULL	Specific serialized unit this tag is affixed to, if applicable
EPCCode	VARCHAR(50)	No	—	Electronic Product Code stored on the tag
TagType	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Passive or Active
AssignedDate	DATE	No	—	Date the tag was affixed
Constraints: UQ_ItemRFID_EPCCode — unique (EPCCode).
Sample data:
Id	Item Ref.	Serial Ref.	EPC Code	Tag Type
1	Toyota Forklift 3 Ton	SN-FLT-22190	E200-3412-0891-4471	Active
2	Siemens MRI Scanner 1.5T	SN-MRI-04471	E200-3412-0891-9902	Active
7.4 Document Attachment
7.4.1 Table: ItemDocument
Purpose: attaches supporting files — datasheets, safety data sheets, compliance certificates, images, manuals — to an item, with version tracking so a superseded certificate remains available for audit rather than being overwritten.
Primary Key: ItemDocumentId (BIGINT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: ItemId → ItemMaster
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ItemDocumentId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ItemId	BIGINT	No	—	Item this document belongs to
DocumentType	VARCHAR(30)	No	—	Datasheet, MSDS, ComplianceCertificate, Image, UserManual, or Warranty
FileName	VARCHAR(255)	No	—	Original file name
FileURL	VARCHAR(500)	No	—	Storage location reference
Version	VARCHAR(10)	No	'1.0'	Document version
UploadedDate	DATE	No	—	Date uploaded
Business rules: a new upload of the same DocumentType for the same item creates a new row with an incremented Version rather than overwriting FileURL, consistent with the never-hard-delete principle (Chapter 8).
Sample data:
Id	Item Ref.	Document Type	File Name	Version
1	Siemens MRI Scanner 1.5T	ComplianceCertificate	MRI-Calibration-Cert-2026.pdf	1.0
2	Bashundhara OPC Cement 50 KG	MSDS	Bashundhara-Cement-MSDS.pdf	2.1
3	Dell Latitude 7450	Datasheet	Dell-Latitude-7450-Spec.pdf	1.0
4	Paracetamol 500mg	ComplianceCertificate	Paracetamol-BatchRelease-A12.pdf	1.0
7.5 Worked Example: Hospital — Medicine and MRI Machine, Same Eight Tables
The hospital scenario from Section 5.1 is a good stress test because it puts a batch-and-expiry item and a serial-and-calibration asset through the same schema side by side.
7.5.1 Paracetamol 500mg (Medicine)
•	ItemMaster: IsLotControlled = 1, IsSerialControlled = 0, CanStock = 1.
•	ItemQCProfile: mandatory Pharmaceutical Batch Release Test, every batch.
•	ItemLot: one row per batch, each with its own ExpiryDate and QCStatus — lot PARA-2026-A13 is Pending and cannot yet be issued (Section 7.2.1).
•	ItemDocument: the batch-release certificate attached per lot via DocumentType = ComplianceCertificate.
7.5.2 Siemens MRI Scanner 1.5T (Asset)
•	ItemMaster: IsSerialControlled = 1, CanStock = 1 until capitalized, CanManufacture = 0.
•	ItemSerial: one row for the specific unit, carrying LastCalibrationDate and NextCalibrationDueDate.
•	AssetMaster (Chapter 6): linked via ItemSerial.LinkedAssetId once capitalized, carrying acquisition cost and depreciation schedule.
•	ItemRFID: an active tag on the specific ItemSerialId, for asset-location tracking within the hospital.
•	ItemDocument: the calibration certificate, versioned every time the unit is recalibrated.
Notice that no new table was needed to support either scenario — the medicine and the MRI machine both go through ItemMaster, an ItemQCProfile-driven quality gate, and either ItemLot or ItemSerial depending on IsLotControlled/IsSerialControlled, exactly as designed in Chapter 4.
7.6 Chapter Summary
Eight tables in this chapter — the QC template/parameter/profile trio, ItemLot and ItemSerial for traceability, ItemBarcode and ItemRFID for identification, and ItemDocument for supporting files — complete the operational layer on top of the classification (Chapter 4), unit (Chapter 5), and functional mapping (Chapter 6) tables already specified. Chapter 8 now turns to governance: the approval workflow, audit trail, version control, and lifecycle states that control how an item moves from Draft to Active and beyond.
 
8. Approval Workflow, Audit Trail, Version Control, Item Lifecycle
Every prior chapter has forward-referenced this one: “never hard-deleted,” “creates a new version row rather than an UPDATE,” “cannot move directly from Draft to Active.” This chapter makes those promises concrete — the state machine an item moves through, the workflow that gates each transition, the audit log that survives every change, and the version history that lets any past state be reconstructed exactly.
8.1 Item Lifecycle State Machine
ItemMaster.ItemStatus (Section 4.10) moves through a fixed set of states. No status is ever skipped, and only Draft and PendingApproval permit a return path:
Draft ────────→ PendingApproval ────────→ Active ────────→ Inactive
  ↑                        │                                              │
  └──── Rejected ────────────┘                                              ↓
                                                                    Obsolete  (terminal)
State definitions and permitted next states:
State	Meaning	Permitted Next State(s)
Draft	Being created or edited; not visible to any transactional module	PendingApproval
PendingApproval	Submitted; moving through ApprovalStep sequence (Section 8.2)	Active (on full approval), Rejected (on any step rejection)
Rejected	An approver declined the request; item returns to Draft for correction	Draft
Active	Transactable — may appear on purchase, sales, and manufacturing documents per its CanPurchase/CanSell/CanManufacture flags	Inactive, Obsolete
Inactive	Temporarily withdrawn from use (e.g. seasonal item, supplier issue); reversible	Active, Obsolete
Obsolete	Permanently retired; terminal state	None — terminal by design; a mistaken obsolescence is corrected via the audited manual-correction path (Section 8.3), not a normal lifecycle transition
Inactive and Obsolete are deliberately distinct, matching the exception “Obsolete vs inactive items” flagged in Chapter 9. Inactive is a switch a category manager can flip back; Obsolete requires the ItemObsolescence record described in Section 8.5 and, if the item has stock on hand, an explicit disposition decision.
8.2 Approval Workflow
An item cannot reach Active status by direct edit. It passes through a named workflow — an ordered sequence of approval steps, each requiring a specific role's sign-off — selected by the item's category (or overridden per item, mirroring the GLMapping/ItemQCProfile override pattern already used twice in this document).
8.2.1 Table: ApprovalWorkflowTemplate
Primary Key: WorkflowTemplateId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: ItemCategoryId → ItemCategory (nullable — NULL means this template must be assigned per item)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
WorkflowTemplateId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
TemplateCode	VARCHAR(30)	No	—	Unique machine code, e.g. ICT-ASSET
TemplateName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display name
ItemCategoryId	INT	Yes	NULL	Category this workflow defaults to, if any
Sample data:
Id	TemplateCode	TemplateName	Default Category
1	STANDARD-ITEM	Standard Two-Step Approval	— (default fallback)
2	ICT-ASSET	ICT Equipment Three-Step Approval	ICT Equipment
3	PHARMA-ITEM	Pharmaceutical Item Approval	— (assigned per item)
4	CONSTRUCTION-MAT	Construction Material Approval	Construction Material
8.2.2 Table: ApprovalStep
Primary Key: ApprovalStepId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: WorkflowTemplateId → ApprovalWorkflowTemplate
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ApprovalStepId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
WorkflowTemplateId	INT	No	—	Parent workflow
StepOrder	INT	No	—	Sequence position, starting at 1
ApproverRole	VARCHAR(50)	No	—	Role required to act on this step, e.g. Category Manager
IsMandatory	BIT	No	1	If 0, this step may be auto-skipped when no user holds the role
Constraints: UQ_ApprovalStep_TemplateOrder — unique (WorkflowTemplateId, StepOrder).
Sample data (ICT-ASSET workflow, WorkflowTemplateId = 2):
Id	Order	Approver Role	Mandatory
1	1	Category Manager	Yes
2	2	IT Security Review	Yes
3	3	Finance Controller	Yes
8.2.3 Table: ItemApprovalRequest
Primary Key: RequestId (BIGINT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: ItemId → ItemMaster; WorkflowTemplateId → ApprovalWorkflowTemplate
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
RequestId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ItemId	BIGINT	No	—	Item under review
WorkflowTemplateId	INT	No	—	Workflow governing this request
RequestedBy	INT	No	—	FK to UserAccount
RequestedDate	DATETIME2(3)	No	—	Submission timestamp
CurrentStepOrder	INT	No	1	Which ApprovalStep is currently pending
OverallStatus	VARCHAR(20)	No	'Pending'	Pending, Approved, or Rejected
Business rules: ItemMaster.ItemStatus moves to Active automatically, in the same transaction, the moment the final step's ItemApprovalAction is recorded as Approved.
Sample data — the Lenovo ThinkPad X1 Carbon from Chapter 4's sample data, currently sitting in PendingApproval at Step 2 of the ICT-ASSET workflow:
Id	Item Ref.	Workflow	Current Step	Status
1	Lenovo ThinkPad X1 Carbon (ItemId 7)	ICT-ASSET	2 – IT Security Review	Pending
8.2.4 Table: ItemApprovalAction
Primary Key: ActionId (BIGINT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: RequestId → ItemApprovalRequest
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ActionId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
RequestId	BIGINT	No	—	Parent approval request
StepOrder	INT	No	—	Which step this action resolves
ActionedBy	INT	No	—	FK to UserAccount
ActionDate	DATETIME2(3)	No	—	Timestamp of the decision
Decision	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Approved or Rejected
Comments	VARCHAR(1000)	Yes	NULL	Reviewer notes, mandatory in the application layer when Decision = Rejected
Sample data (Step 1 of the Lenovo ThinkPad request, already approved; Step 2 still awaiting action):
Id	Request Ref.	Step	Decision	Comments
1	Lenovo ThinkPad request	1 – Category Manager	Approved	Matches standing laptop spec
8.3 Audit Trail
8.3.1 Table: ItemAuditLog
Purpose: a field-level, append-only record of every change to ItemMaster or ItemAttribute, independent of the CreatedBy/ModifiedBy columns on the row itself (Section 4.1), which only capture the most recent change. This table answers “what did this look like on any given date,” not just “who touched it last.”
Primary Key: AuditLogId (BIGINT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: ItemId → ItemMaster
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
AuditLogId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ItemId	BIGINT	No	—	Item that changed
TableName	VARCHAR(50)	No	—	ItemMaster or ItemAttribute
FieldName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Column or AttributeCode that changed
OldValue	NVARCHAR(1000)	Yes	NULL	Value before the change
NewValue	NVARCHAR(1000)	Yes	NULL	Value after the change
ChangeType	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Insert, Update, or SoftDelete
ChangedBy	INT	No	—	FK to UserAccount
ChangedDate	DATETIME2(3)	No	SYSUTCDATETIME()	Timestamp
Business rules: written exclusively by a database trigger on ItemMaster and ItemAttribute, never by application code directly — this closes the gap where a direct SQL edit could bypass an application-level audit call.
Sample data:
Id	Item Ref.	Field	Old → New	Type
1	Lenovo ThinkPad X1 Carbon	ItemStatus	Draft → PendingApproval	Update
2	Dell Latitude 7450	ItemStatus	PendingApproval → Active	Update
3	Bashundhara OPC Cement 50 KG	RAM (n/a example)	—	Insert
8.4 Version Control
8.4.1 Table: ItemVersion
Purpose: a full snapshot of an item's state at each approved revision, so that “what did this item's attributes look like on the date of a given transaction” (Chapter 1's traceability objective) can be answered exactly, not reconstructed from the audit log.
Primary Key: ItemVersionId (BIGINT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: ItemId → ItemMaster; ApprovalRequestId → ItemApprovalRequest (nullable — NULL for the initial creation version)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ItemVersionId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ItemId	BIGINT	No	—	Item this version belongs to
VersionNumber	INT	No	—	Matches ItemMaster.VersionNumber (Section 4.10) at the moment of snapshot
SnapshotJson	NVARCHAR(MAX)	No	—	Full serialized state of ItemMaster and its ItemAttribute rows at this version
ChangeReason	VARCHAR(500)	Yes	NULL	Free-text reason for the revision
ApprovalRequestId	BIGINT	Yes	NULL	The approval that activated this version, if any
Constraints: UQ_ItemVersion_ItemVersionNumber — unique (ItemId, VersionNumber).
Business rules: a new ItemVersion row is written every time an Active item's ItemMaster or ItemAttribute data changes and is re-approved; ItemMaster.VersionNumber increments in the same transaction. Pre-Active (Draft/PendingApproval) edits do not create version rows — versioning starts once an item has gone live.
Sample data:
Id	Item Ref.	Version	Change Reason
1	Dell Latitude 7450	1	Initial approval and activation
2	Dell Latitude 7450	2	RAM configuration corrected from 16GB to 32GB post-launch
8.5 Item Obsolescence
8.5.1 Table: ItemObsolescence
Purpose: records why and when an item was retired, and what (if anything) replaces it — the detail that distinguishes a considered obsolescence decision from simply flipping IsActive off.
Primary Key: ObsolescenceId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: ItemId → ItemMaster; ReplacementItemId → ItemMaster (nullable)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ObsolescenceId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ItemId	BIGINT	No	—	The item being retired
ReplacementItemId	BIGINT	Yes	NULL	The item that supersedes it, if any
ObsoleteDate	DATE	No	—	Effective date of retirement
Reason	VARCHAR(500)	No	—	Why the item was retired
Business rules:
•	Setting ItemMaster.ItemStatus to Obsolete requires a corresponding ItemObsolescence row in the same transaction — the status change is rejected by the application layer if one is not supplied.
•	If any WarehouseItem row for the item still shows QuantityOnHand > 0 at the time of obsolescence, the request is flagged for manual disposition review rather than silently proceeding.
•	ReplacementItemId, when set, is surfaced automatically to procurement and sales screens that reference the obsolete item, redirecting new demand — without altering any historical transaction, which continues to reference the original ItemId.
Sample data:
Id	Retired Item	Replacement	Reason
1	Cisco Catalyst 9200 Switch (legacy)	Cisco ISR4331 Router	End-of-sale by manufacturer; superseded model adopted
8.6 Chapter Summary
This chapter turned four forward-referenced promises into implementable tables: a strict item-status state machine (Section 8.1); a role-based, multi-step approval workflow that is the only path to Active status (Section 8.2); an append-only, trigger-written audit log independent of the row-level CreatedBy/ModifiedBy columns (Section 8.3); full-snapshot version history for reconstructing any past state (Section 8.4); and a deliberate obsolescence record rather than a bare status flip (Section 8.5). Chapter 9 now catalogues the exception scenarios — duplicate codes, circular classifications, item merge/split, and more — that this governance layer exists to handle.
 
9. Exceptions & Edge Cases
Every chapter so far has forward-referenced this one at the moment a design decision was really about handling an exception rather than the happy path. This chapter collects all twenty of them in one place, grouped by theme, each stated as a scenario, a resolution, and a pointer to the exact table and rule that implements it. Several resolutions here are the first formal statement of a rule only implied earlier — those are marked as amendments.
9.1 Identity & Classification Exceptions
9.1.1 Duplicate Item Codes Across Business Entities
Scenario: Garments and ICT independently create an item for “office chair” before realizing it's the same physical product, or worse, two genuinely different items collide on the same intuitive code.
Resolution:
•	ItemCode is globally unique across all of ItemMaster (UQ_ItemMaster_Code, Section 4.10) — there is no per-unit code namespace, so true duplicates are rejected by the database at creation time, not caught later by reconciliation.
•	If an unit's legacy system already used a conflicting code for what turns out to be a genuinely different item, that unit's alias is stored in BusinessUnitItem.LocalItemCode (Section 5.3) rather than forcing a change to the canonical, group-wide ItemCode.
9.1.2 Circular Parent-Child Classifications
Scenario: A hierarchy reference loops back on itself — either the classification chain or a bill of materials.
Resolution:
•	ItemCategory → ItemGroup → ItemSubGroup → ItemFamily (Section 4.3–4.6) are four distinct tables, each referencing only its immediate parent's table. Because no table in this chain references itself, a classification cycle is structurally impossible — this is a property of the design, not a runtime check.
•	Company → BusinessUnit (Section 5.2) is a fixed two-level foreign key, not a self-reference, so a circular company/unit structure is structurally impossible in the same way the classification chain above is — this reflects a deliberate improvement made during Chapter 5's design (see Appendix A.1) over an earlier self-referencing approach. BOMComponent (Section 6.5.2), which does link ItemMaster back to itself through BillOfMaterial, is the one genuine self-reference left in the schema, and is validated on write by a recursive check that rejects any insert/update that would make an item its own ancestor.
9.1.3 Item Code Renaming Restrictions
Scenario: A typo in ItemCode is discovered, or a business wants to rename an item to match a new internal convention.
Resolution:
•	ItemCode may be freely corrected while ItemStatus = Draft (Section 8.1), since no transaction can reference it yet.
•	Once any transaction, SupplierPrice, WarehouseItem, or ItemLot/ItemSerial row references the ItemId, ItemCode is locked (Section 4.10's validation rule) — renaming is rejected outright, not merely discouraged, because ItemCode may already be printed on physical labels and referenced in external systems via EDI.
•	A genuinely necessary rename after go-live follows the merge path in Section 9.1.4 instead: create a new ItemMaster row with the corrected code and obsolete the old one with a ReplacementItemId link.
9.1.4 Item Merge and Split Scenarios
Scenario: Two items are discovered to be duplicates and need to become one; or a single item needs to split into distinct variants (e.g. an undifferentiated “Cotton Fabric” item is discovered to actually be two GSM weights that should never have shared a code).
Resolution — merge:
•	The losing ItemId is set to Obsolete via ItemObsolescence with ReplacementItemId pointing to the surviving ItemId (Section 8.5).
•	A merge transaction reassigns BusinessUnitItem, SupplierItem, and current WarehouseItem balances from the losing ItemId to the surviving ItemId.
•	Historical transactions are never rewritten to point at the surviving ItemId — they keep referencing the original ItemId permanently, per Section 9.7.1.
Resolution — split:
•	New ItemMaster rows are created for each resulting variant; the original is marked Obsolete.
•	Because ItemObsolescence.ReplacementItemId is a single foreign key, a split with more than one successor records its primary/default replacement there and documents the remaining successors in the Reason field; a future ItemObsolescenceReplacement many-to-many table is a known, low-risk extension point if splits become frequent enough to warrant first-class support.
9.2 Sourcing & Multi-Vendor Exceptions
9.2.1 Same SKU Supplied by Multiple Vendors
Scenario: The Dell Latitude 7450 is available from Dell directly, from Computer Source, and from Smart Tech, each at a different part number and price.
Resolution: fully solved already by SupplierItem and SupplierPrice (Sections 6.2.2–6.2.3) — one ItemMaster row, many SupplierItem rows, IsPreferred marking the default. No additional mechanism is needed; this exception is listed here for completeness against the original requirements catalogue.
9.2.2 Multi-Currency Purchasing
Scenario: The same item is purchased in USD from one supplier and BDT from another.
Resolution:
•	SupplierPrice.Currency (Section 6.2.3) stores the price exactly as quoted by that supplier — no forced conversion at storage time.
•	Currency conversion at the moment of a purchase transaction is a finance-module concern (exchange rate tables), explicitly out of scope per Section 1.2; this document only guarantees the source-of-truth price and currency are captured without loss.
9.2.3 Multiple Tax Jurisdictions
Scenario: The same item is taxed at 15% VAT in Bangladesh and at a different rate or type entirely if sourced from or sold into another jurisdiction.
Resolution:
•	TaxMapping (Section 6.4.2) already allows multiple rows per item or category, one per Jurisdiction.
•	Amendment: Section 6.4.2 did not state a uniqueness constraint explicitly — it is added here: UQ_TaxMapping_TargetJurisdiction — unique (ItemCategoryId, ItemId, Jurisdiction), preventing two conflicting active rates for the same item in the same jurisdiction at the same time.
9.3 Multi-Entity & Ownership Exceptions
9.3.1 One Item Belonging to Multiple Business Units
Scenario: A laptop or a switch is shared across five unrelated divisions.
Resolution: solved by BusinessUnitItem (Section 5.3); listed here for completeness — see Section 5.3's worked Dell Laptop / Cisco Switch examples.
9.3.2 Intercompany and Inter-Unit Transfers
Scenario: 100 laptops purchased centrally by WeavoGo's Corporate unit are physically transferred to its ICT, Garments, and Construction units — all under the same Company — while a separate transfer moves stock from WeavoGo to TISWL, a genuinely different Company (Section 5.2.1).
Resolution:
•	The physical movement itself is a transaction (out of scope, Section 1.2), but Company vs. BusinessUnit (Section 5.2) gives it the master data it needs to post correctly either way: a transfer between two BusinessUnits under the same CompanyId is an inter-unit stock move; a transfer where the sending and receiving BusinessUnit have different CompanyId values is a true intercompany transaction requiring an intercompany receivable/payable pair, not just a warehouse-to-warehouse stock move.
•	GLMapping (Section 6.4.1) is defined per BusinessUnitId, so both cases post using each unit's own GL codes; the distinction above only changes whether the posting also creates an intercompany due-to/due-from pair, which is a rule the transaction layer applies based on comparing the two BusinessUnit rows' CompanyId, not something ItemMaster or WarehouseItem need to know about.
•	WarehouseItem (Section 6.1.2) tracks balances per Warehouse, and Warehouse.BusinessUnitId (Section 6.1.1) identifies which unit owns each warehouse — sufficient for a transfer transaction to decrement one unit's balance and increment another's without any change to ItemMaster, regardless of whether a Company boundary was crossed.
9.3.3 Cross-Company Visibility Rules
Scenario: An item should be visible and transactable in some business units but completely hidden from others — not just restricted, but absent from pick-lists entirely, whether those units sit under the same Company or different ones.
Resolution: BusinessUnitItem (Section 5.3) is default-deny — an item is invisible to a business unit unless an active, IsAuthorized = 1 row exists, and this holds identically whether the comparison is between two units of the same Company or across different Companies. There is no global “visible everywhere” flag on ItemMaster itself, which is what keeps this rule enforceable in exactly one place.
9.4 Unit of Measure Exceptions
9.4.1 UOM Conversion Conflicts
Scenario: The generic UOMConversion table (Section 5.5) says 1 Box = 1 Piece as a placeholder default, but a specific item is actually packed 5-per-box.
Resolution:
•	Formal precedence rule (first noted informally in Section 5.6, restated here as binding): whenever an ItemPackaging row exists for a given item and packaging level, it overrides the generic UOMConversion default for that item. UOMConversion is a fallback for items with no item-specific packaging defined, never an override of ItemPackaging.
•	Conversions across incompatible UOMType values (Weight to Length, for example) are rejected at the application layer regardless of which table they originate from (Section 5.5).
9.5 Lifecycle & Attribute Exceptions
9.5.1 Obsolete vs. Inactive Items
Scenario: A category manager wants to temporarily stop selling a seasonal item versus permanently retiring a discontinued one.
Resolution: fully solved by the Inactive/Obsolete distinction in Section 8.1 and the ItemObsolescence table in Section 8.5; listed here for completeness against the original requirements catalogue.
9.5.2 Revision-Controlled Materials
Scenario: A fabric formulation changes slightly (a dye supplier substitution) versus changing significantly (a different fiber blend that affects fit and performance) — should either become a new ItemVersion or a wholly new ItemMaster row?
Resolution — decision rule:
•	If a downstream process (a customer, a compliance certificate, a BOM that consumes it) would need to know the change happened, it is a new ItemMaster row, linked from the old one via ItemObsolescence, following the same pattern as Section 9.1.4.
•	If the change is corrective or non-substantive to anyone downstream (a typo in the description, a GSM figure corrected after re-measurement), it is a new ItemVersion row (Section 8.4) against the same ItemId.
9.5.3 Attribute Template Changes After Transactions
Scenario: A new mandatory attribute (say, a compliance certificate number) is added to the Laptop template after hundreds of laptops are already Active.
Resolution:
•	Already-Active items are not retroactively invalidated — the new TemplateAttribute.IsRequired = 1 (Section 4.9) applies to items created or re-approved after the change.
•	A scheduled reconciliation job flags existing Active items missing a newly-mandatory attribute for review, rather than silently blocking their transactions or silently ignoring the gap.
•	As already stated in Section 4.7: an AttributeDefinition's DataType itself can never change once any ItemAttribute references it — only which template requires which attribute can change, not what type an existing attribute holds.
9.5.4 Seasonal and Buyer-Specific Items
Scenario: An item is only sold during a particular season, or is exclusive to one customer's private-label line.
Resolution:
•	Seasonality toggles ItemStatus between Active and Inactive (Section 8.1) without triggering full obsolescence — the item returns exactly as it was when reactivated.
•	Buyer exclusivity is enforced the same way unit visibility is enforced (Section 9.3.3): CustomerItem.IsAuthorized (Section 6.3.1) is default-deny, so an item with no CustomerItem row for a given customer simply cannot be sold to them.
9.6 Inventory Traceability Exceptions
9.6.1 Lot-Controlled and Serial-Controlled Inventory Together
Scenario: A regulated electronic device needs both a manufacturing batch/lot code and an individual unit serial number — IsLotControlled and IsSerialControlled are both true for the same item.
Resolution: both flags may legitimately be true simultaneously. Amendment: ItemSerial (Section 7.2.2) is extended with an optional ItemLotId foreign key to ItemLot, so a specific serialized unit can be traced back to the manufacturing batch it came from, without either table needing to duplicate the other's data.
9.6.2 Batch Expiration
Scenario: Multiple lots of the same medicine sit in stock with different expiry dates; issuance must respect expiry order.
Resolution: ItemLot.ExpiryDate (Section 7.2.1) already captures the date; amendment — issuance transactions for any item with IsLotControlled = 1 and a non-NULL ExpiryDate must select the earliest-expiring eligible lot first (FEFO — first-expired-first-out), enforced at the transaction layer rather than left to picker discretion.
9.6.3 Barcode Duplication
Scenario: A printed barcode is damaged and must be reprinted; or, rarely, a legacy barcode standard recycles a value.
Resolution: ItemBarcode.BarcodeValue is globally unique (Section 7.3.1) and never reused across items. A reprint creates a new ItemBarcode row with IsPrimary = 1 and sets the damaged row's IsActive = 0 — the old value is retired, not deleted, so historical scans referencing it remain traceable.
9.6.4 RFID Uniqueness
Scenario: A physical RFID tag is removed from a disposed asset and the EPC needs to be reused on a new unit.
Resolution: the same pattern as barcode reprinting — ItemRFID.EPCCode is globally unique (Section 7.3.2); the old assignment's IsActive is set to 0 rather than the row being deleted, and a new ItemRFID row captures the new assignment, preserving a complete history of which physical asset wore that tag and when.
9.7 Data Integrity Exceptions
9.7.1 Historical Transaction Integrity
Scenario: The capstone rule underlying nearly every resolution above: once any transaction has referenced an ItemId, an ItemAttribute value, a SupplierPrice, a WarehouseItem lot, or any other row in this schema, that reference must remain resolvable exactly as it was at the time, indefinitely.
Resolution — the mechanisms already specified across this document, restated together as the single principle they implement:
•	Nothing is ever hard-deleted (IsDeleted / IsActive flags throughout, Section 4.1).
•	Changes to an Active item after go-live create a new ItemVersion row rather than overwriting fields in place (Section 8.4).
•	Field-level history is independently captured in ItemAuditLog regardless of whether a formal version was cut (Section 8.3).
•	Merges, splits, and obsolescence redirect future demand via ReplacementItemId, but never rewrite which ItemId a past transaction actually referenced (Section 9.1.4).
If a future requirement seems to demand editing history — correcting a transaction that turns out to have used the wrong item — the correct pattern is a new, offsetting transaction dated today that references today's understanding, not an edit to the original record. This is the one rule in this document with no configurable exception.
9.8 Chapter Summary
Twenty exception scenarios, seven of them (Sections 9.1.2, 9.2.3, 9.4.1, 9.6.1, 9.6.2) resolved here with an explicit amendment to an earlier chapter's schema or rule, and the rest resolved entirely by mechanisms already in place by Chapter 8. Chapter 10 now turns outward to integration: the API payload examples and SQL table structures a developer needs to actually build against this specification.
 
10. API Payload Examples & SQL Table Structures
This chapter turns the schema from Chapters 4–9 into two concrete developer artifacts: the JSON contract integration developers build against, and the T-SQL DDL backend developers run to create the database. Every field name below matches its schema table exactly — there is no translation layer between what this document calls a column and what the API calls a field.
10.1 API Design Conventions
•	Base path: /api/v1/ — all endpoints in this chapter are versioned; a breaking change ships as /api/v2/ rather than mutating v1 in place.
•	Authentication: bearer token in the Authorization header on every request (mechanism defined outside this document's scope).
•	All timestamps are ISO 8601 UTC. All monetary and quantity fields are returned as numbers, never pre-formatted strings.
•	Write endpoints (POST/PATCH) are idempotent when an Idempotency-Key header is supplied, to make safe client-side retries possible.
•	List endpoints are always paginated; there is no unpaginated “get all items” endpoint given the catalogue can run into the hundreds of thousands of rows.
10.2 Endpoint Catalogue
Method & Path	Purpose	Section
POST /items	Create a new item in Draft status	10.3
GET /items/{itemId}	Retrieve full item detail	10.4
GET /items	Search/list items with filters	10.5
PATCH /items/{itemId}	Partially update an existing item	10.6
POST /items/{itemId}/submit	Submit a Draft item for approval	10.7
POST /items/{itemId}/approval-actions	Record an approve/reject decision on the current step	10.7
GET /items/{itemId}/versions	List version history (Section 8.4)	—
POST /items/{itemId}/business-units	Authorize the item for a business unit (Section 5.3)	—
10.3 Create Item
POST /api/v1/items — creates a new item in Draft status. Dynamic attributes are supplied inline by AttributeCode; the server resolves each against the item's effective AttributeTemplate (Section 4.9) and rejects the request if a required attribute is missing or a supplied value doesn't match its DataType.
Request:
POST /api/v1/items
Content-Type: application/json
 
{
  "itemCode": "ICT-LTP-000024",
  "itemName": "Dell Latitude 7450 Laptop",
  "itemFamilyId": 6,
  "baseUOMId": 1,
  "canPurchase": true,
  "canSell": false,
  "canManufacture": false,
  "canStock": true,
  "canTransfer": true,
  "isSerialControlled": true,
  "isLotControlled": false,
  "attributes": [
    { "attributeCode": "BRAND", "value": "Dell" },
    { "attributeCode": "MODEL", "value": "Latitude 7450" },
    { "attributeCode": "CPU", "value": "Intel Core Ultra 7" },
    { "attributeCode": "RAM", "value": 32 },
    { "attributeCode": "WARRANTY", "value": 3 }
  ],
  "businessUnitIds": [6, 10]
}
Response (201 Created):
{
  "itemId": 24,
  "itemCode": "ICT-LTP-000024",
  "itemStatus": "Draft",
  "versionNumber": 1,
  "createdBy": 101,
  "createdDate": "2026-08-02T09:15:32.000Z",
  "_links": {
    "self": "/api/v1/items/24",
    "submitForApproval": "/api/v1/items/24/submit"
  }
}
A 201 response never includes the resolved classification ancestry (category/group/sub-group) or QC profile — those are read back via GET (Section 10.4) to keep the create response fast and avoid returning derived data the client didn't ask for.
10.4 Get Item Detail
GET /api/v1/items/{itemId} — returns the full, denormalized representation of an Active item, including its classification ancestry, resolved attributes, business unit authorizations, and packaging hierarchy.
GET /api/v1/items/6
 
{
  "itemId": 6,
  "itemCode": "ICT-LTP-000006",
  "itemName": "Dell Latitude 7450 Laptop",
  "itemStatus": "Active",
  "versionNumber": 1,
  "classification": {
    "categoryId": 6,  "categoryName": "ICT Equipment",
    "groupId": 6,     "groupName": "Computer",
    "subGroupId": 7,  "subGroupName": "Laptop",
    "familyId": 6,    "familyName": "Dell"
  },
  "flags": {
    "canPurchase": true, "canSell": false, "canManufacture": false,
    "canStock": true, "canTransfer": true,
    "isSerialControlled": true, "isLotControlled": false
  },
  "attributes": [
    { "attributeCode": "BRAND", "attributeName": "Brand", "value": "Dell" },
    { "attributeCode": "MODEL", "attributeName": "Model", "value": "Latitude 7450" },
    { "attributeCode": "RAM", "attributeName": "RAM", "value": 32, "unitOfMeasure": "GB" }
  ],
  "businessUnits": [
    { "businessUnitId": 10, "unitName": "Corporate Services", "isAuthorized": true },
    { "businessUnitId": 6,  "unitName": "ICT & Software Division", "isAuthorized": true }
  ],
  "packaging": [
    { "level": 1, "levelName": "Each", "uom": "PCS", "qtyPerParentLevel": 1 },
    { "level": 3, "levelName": "Carton", "uom": "CTN", "qtyPerParentLevel": 5 }
  ]
}
10.5 Search / List Items
GET /api/v1/items — filterable, paginated. Common query parameters:
Parameter	Example	Description
categoryCode	ICT	Filter by ItemCategory.CategoryCode
status	Active	Filter by ItemStatus
businessEntityId	6	Only items authorized for this unit (Section 5.3)
search	“laptop”	Free-text match against ItemCode and ItemName
page / pageSize	1 / 20	Pagination controls; pageSize capped at 100
GET /api/v1/items?categoryCode=ICT&status=Active&page=1&pageSize=20
 
{
  "items": [
    { "itemId": 6, "itemCode": "ICT-LTP-000006", "itemName": "Dell Latitude 7450 Laptop", "itemStatus": "Active" },
    { "itemId": 8, "itemCode": "ICT-SRV-000007", "itemName": "HPE ProLiant DL380 Gen11 Server", "itemStatus": "Active" }
  ],
  "pagination": { "page": 1, "pageSize": 20, "totalItems": 2, "totalPages": 1 }
}
10.6 Update Item (Partial)
PATCH /api/v1/items/{itemId} — supports partial updates. Updating an Active item's fields does not apply the change directly; it opens a new Draft-style edit that must pass approval again and, once approved, is written as a new ItemVersion (Section 8.4) rather than an in-place overwrite.
PATCH /api/v1/items/6
Content-Type: application/json
 
{
  "attributes": [
    { "attributeCode": "RAM", "value": 32 }
  ],
  "changeReason": "RAM configuration corrected from 16GB to 32GB post-launch"
}
{
  "itemId": 6,
  "itemStatus": "PendingApproval",
  "pendingVersionNumber": 2,
  "approvalRequestId": 481,
  "_links": { "approvalRequest": "/api/v1/approval-requests/481" }
}
10.7 Submit & Approve
POST /api/v1/items/24/submit
 
{ "workflowTemplateCode": "ICT-ASSET" }
 
→ 202 Accepted
{ "requestId": 512, "currentStepOrder": 1, "overallStatus": "Pending" }
POST /api/v1/approval-requests/512/approval-actions
 
{
  "stepOrder": 1,
  "decision": "Approved",
  "comments": "Matches standing laptop specification"
}
 
→ 200 OK
{ "requestId": 512, "currentStepOrder": 2, "overallStatus": "Pending" }
10.8 Error Response Format
Every non-2xx response uses the same envelope, so client error handling never needs endpoint-specific branching:
{
  "error": {
    "code": "ITEM_NOT_AUTHORIZED_FOR_ENTITY",
    "message": "Item 6 is not authorized for business unit 13.",
    "field": null,
    "traceId": "8f14e45f-ea08-4c34-9a1e-2c3b1f0a9d77"
  }
}
Error Code	HTTP Status	Meaning
ITEM_CODE_DUPLICATE	409	ItemCode already exists (Section 4.10)
ATTRIBUTE_REQUIRED_MISSING	422	A mandatory TemplateAttribute has no value (Section 4.9)
ITEM_NOT_AUTHORIZED_FOR_ENTITY	403	No active BusinessUnitItem row (Section 5.3)
ITEM_CODE_LOCKED	409	Attempted rename after transactional history exists (Section 4.10)
INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION	409	Requested ItemStatus change is not a permitted transition (Section 8.1)
QC_PROFILE_NOT_SATISFIED	422	Mandatory QC parameters not yet passed (Section 7.1.3)
10.9 SQL Table Structures
Full T-SQL DDL for the nine core tables from Chapter 4 is given below, translated directly from their schema tables with no changes. Every other table in Chapters 5–9 follows the identical translation pattern — column list, PRIMARY KEY, FOREIGN KEY, and CHECK constraints exactly as specified in that table's schema listing — and is not repeated here in full to keep this chapter to a manageable length; Section 10.9.10 gives one additional worked example (WarehouseItem) explicitly to demonstrate the pattern carries over unchanged into the supporting tables.
10.9.1 ItemCategory
CREATE TABLE dbo.ItemCategory (
    ItemCategoryId   INT IDENTITY(1,1)   NOT NULL,
    CategoryCode     VARCHAR(10)         NOT NULL,
    CategoryName     VARCHAR(100)        NOT NULL,
    Nature           VARCHAR(20)         NOT NULL,
    Description      VARCHAR(500)        NULL,
    DisplayOrder     INT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    CreatedBy        INT                 NOT NULL,
    CreatedDate      DATETIME2(3)        NOT NULL DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME()),
    ModifiedBy       INT                 NULL,
    ModifiedDate     DATETIME2(3)        NULL,
    IsActive         BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    IsDeleted        BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    RowVersion       ROWVERSION          NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT PK_ItemCategory PRIMARY KEY CLUSTERED (ItemCategoryId),
    CONSTRAINT UQ_ItemCategory_Code UNIQUE (CategoryCode),
    CONSTRAINT CK_ItemCategory_Nature CHECK (Nature IN ('Material','Goods','Asset','Service'))
);
10.9.2 ItemGroup
CREATE TABLE dbo.ItemGroup (
    ItemGroupId      INT IDENTITY(1,1)   NOT NULL,
    ItemCategoryId   INT                 NOT NULL,
    GroupCode        VARCHAR(10)         NOT NULL,
    GroupName        VARCHAR(100)        NOT NULL,
    Description      VARCHAR(500)        NULL,
    DisplayOrder     INT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    CreatedBy        INT                 NOT NULL,
    CreatedDate      DATETIME2(3)        NOT NULL DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME()),
    ModifiedBy       INT                 NULL,
    ModifiedDate     DATETIME2(3)        NULL,
    IsActive         BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    IsDeleted        BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    RowVersion       ROWVERSION          NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT PK_ItemGroup PRIMARY KEY CLUSTERED (ItemGroupId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_ItemGroup_Category FOREIGN KEY (ItemCategoryId)
        REFERENCES dbo.ItemCategory (ItemCategoryId),
    CONSTRAINT UQ_ItemGroup_CategoryCode UNIQUE (ItemCategoryId, GroupCode)
);
10.9.3 ItemSubGroup
CREATE TABLE dbo.ItemSubGroup (
    ItemSubGroupId   INT IDENTITY(1,1)   NOT NULL,
    ItemGroupId      INT                 NOT NULL,
    SubGroupCode     VARCHAR(10)         NOT NULL,
    SubGroupName     VARCHAR(100)        NOT NULL,
    Description      VARCHAR(500)        NULL,
    DisplayOrder     INT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    CreatedBy        INT                 NOT NULL,
    CreatedDate      DATETIME2(3)        NOT NULL DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME()),
    ModifiedBy       INT                 NULL,
    ModifiedDate     DATETIME2(3)        NULL,
    IsActive         BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    IsDeleted        BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    RowVersion       ROWVERSION          NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT PK_ItemSubGroup PRIMARY KEY CLUSTERED (ItemSubGroupId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_ItemSubGroup_Group FOREIGN KEY (ItemGroupId)
        REFERENCES dbo.ItemGroup (ItemGroupId),
    CONSTRAINT UQ_ItemSubGroup_GroupCode UNIQUE (ItemGroupId, SubGroupCode)
);
10.9.4 ItemFamily
CREATE TABLE dbo.ItemFamily (
    ItemFamilyId                INT IDENTITY(1,1)   NOT NULL,
    ItemSubGroupId               INT                 NOT NULL,
    FamilyCode                   VARCHAR(10)         NOT NULL,
    FamilyName                   VARCHAR(100)        NOT NULL,
    DefaultAttributeTemplateId   INT                 NULL,
    DisplayOrder                 INT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    CreatedBy                    INT                 NOT NULL,
    CreatedDate                  DATETIME2(3)        NOT NULL DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME()),
    ModifiedBy                   INT                 NULL,
    ModifiedDate                 DATETIME2(3)        NULL,
    IsActive                     BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    IsDeleted                    BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    RowVersion                   ROWVERSION          NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT PK_ItemFamily PRIMARY KEY CLUSTERED (ItemFamilyId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_ItemFamily_SubGroup FOREIGN KEY (ItemSubGroupId)
        REFERENCES dbo.ItemSubGroup (ItemSubGroupId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_ItemFamily_Template FOREIGN KEY (DefaultAttributeTemplateId)
        REFERENCES dbo.AttributeTemplate (AttributeTemplateId),
    CONSTRAINT UQ_ItemFamily_SubGroupCode UNIQUE (ItemSubGroupId, FamilyCode)
);
10.9.5 AttributeDefinition
CREATE TABLE dbo.AttributeDefinition (
    AttributeDefinitionId   INT IDENTITY(1,1)   NOT NULL,
    AttributeCode           VARCHAR(50)         NOT NULL,
    AttributeName           VARCHAR(100)        NOT NULL,
    DataType                VARCHAR(20)         NOT NULL,
    UnitOfMeasure           VARCHAR(20)         NULL,
    EnumOptions              NVARCHAR(MAX)       NULL,
    CreatedBy               INT                 NOT NULL,
    CreatedDate              DATETIME2(3)        NOT NULL DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME()),
    ModifiedBy               INT                 NULL,
    ModifiedDate             DATETIME2(3)        NULL,
    IsActive                 BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    IsDeleted                 BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    RowVersion                ROWVERSION          NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT PK_AttributeDefinition PRIMARY KEY CLUSTERED (AttributeDefinitionId),
    CONSTRAINT UQ_AttributeDefinition_Code UNIQUE (AttributeCode),
    CONSTRAINT CK_AttributeDefinition_DataType
        CHECK (DataType IN ('Text','Number','Boolean','Date','Enum')),
    CONSTRAINT CK_AttributeDefinition_EnumOptions
        CHECK (DataType <> 'Enum' OR EnumOptions IS NOT NULL)
);
10.9.6 AttributeTemplate
CREATE TABLE dbo.AttributeTemplate (
    AttributeTemplateId   INT IDENTITY(1,1)   NOT NULL,
    TemplateCode          VARCHAR(30)         NOT NULL,
    TemplateName          VARCHAR(100)        NOT NULL,
    Description           VARCHAR(500)        NULL,
    CreatedBy             INT                 NOT NULL,
    CreatedDate           DATETIME2(3)        NOT NULL DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME()),
    ModifiedBy            INT                 NULL,
    ModifiedDate          DATETIME2(3)        NULL,
    IsActive              BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    IsDeleted             BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    RowVersion            ROWVERSION          NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT PK_AttributeTemplate PRIMARY KEY CLUSTERED (AttributeTemplateId),
    CONSTRAINT UQ_AttributeTemplate_Code UNIQUE (TemplateCode)
);
10.9.7 TemplateAttribute
CREATE TABLE dbo.TemplateAttribute (
    TemplateAttributeId     INT IDENTITY(1,1)   NOT NULL,
    AttributeTemplateId     INT                 NOT NULL,
    AttributeDefinitionId   INT                 NOT NULL,
    IsRequired               BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    DisplayOrder             INT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    DefaultValue              NVARCHAR(200)       NULL,
    CreatedBy                 INT                 NOT NULL,
    CreatedDate                DATETIME2(3)        NOT NULL DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME()),
    ModifiedBy                 INT                 NULL,
    ModifiedDate               DATETIME2(3)        NULL,
    IsActive                   BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    IsDeleted                   BIT                 NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    RowVersion                  ROWVERSION          NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT PK_TemplateAttribute PRIMARY KEY CLUSTERED (TemplateAttributeId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_TemplateAttribute_Template FOREIGN KEY (AttributeTemplateId)
        REFERENCES dbo.AttributeTemplate (AttributeTemplateId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_TemplateAttribute_Definition FOREIGN KEY (AttributeDefinitionId)
        REFERENCES dbo.AttributeDefinition (AttributeDefinitionId),
    CONSTRAINT UQ_TemplateAttribute_TemplateDefinition
        UNIQUE (AttributeTemplateId, AttributeDefinitionId)
);
10.9.8 ItemMaster
CREATE TABLE dbo.ItemMaster (
    ItemId                 BIGINT IDENTITY(1,1)   NOT NULL,
    ItemCode               VARCHAR(30)            NOT NULL,
    ItemName               NVARCHAR(200)          NOT NULL,
    Description             NVARCHAR(1000)         NULL,
    ItemCategoryId          INT                    NOT NULL,
    ItemGroupId              INT                    NOT NULL,
    ItemSubGroupId            INT                    NOT NULL,
    ItemFamilyId               INT                    NOT NULL,
    AttributeTemplateId         INT                    NULL,
    BaseUOMId                    INT                    NOT NULL,
    CanPurchase                   BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    CanSell                        BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    CanManufacture                  BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    CanStock                         BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    CanTransfer                       BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    IsSerialControlled                 BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    IsLotControlled                     BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    ItemStatus                           VARCHAR(20)            NOT NULL DEFAULT ('Draft'),
    VersionNumber                         INT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    CreatedBy               INT                    NOT NULL,
    CreatedDate              DATETIME2(3)           NOT NULL DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME()),
    ModifiedBy                INT                    NULL,
    ModifiedDate               DATETIME2(3)           NULL,
    IsActive                    BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    IsDeleted                    BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    RowVersion                    ROWVERSION             NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT PK_ItemMaster PRIMARY KEY CLUSTERED (ItemId),
    CONSTRAINT UQ_ItemMaster_Code UNIQUE (ItemCode),
    CONSTRAINT FK_ItemMaster_Category FOREIGN KEY (ItemCategoryId) REFERENCES dbo.ItemCategory (ItemCategoryId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_ItemMaster_Group FOREIGN KEY (ItemGroupId) REFERENCES dbo.ItemGroup (ItemGroupId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_ItemMaster_SubGroup FOREIGN KEY (ItemSubGroupId) REFERENCES dbo.ItemSubGroup (ItemSubGroupId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_ItemMaster_Family FOREIGN KEY (ItemFamilyId) REFERENCES dbo.ItemFamily (ItemFamilyId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_ItemMaster_Template FOREIGN KEY (AttributeTemplateId) REFERENCES dbo.AttributeTemplate (AttributeTemplateId),
    CONSTRAINT CK_ItemMaster_Status
        CHECK (ItemStatus IN ('Draft','PendingApproval','Active','Inactive','Obsolete')),
    CONSTRAINT CK_ItemMaster_StockFlags CHECK (CanManufacture = 0 OR CanStock = 1)
);
10.9.9 ItemAttribute
CREATE TABLE dbo.ItemAttribute (
    ItemAttributeId         BIGINT IDENTITY(1,1)   NOT NULL,
    ItemId                    BIGINT                 NOT NULL,
    AttributeDefinitionId       INT                    NOT NULL,
    ValueText                     NVARCHAR(500)          NULL,
    ValueNumber                     DECIMAL(18,4)          NULL,
    ValueDate                         DATE                    NULL,
    ValueBoolean                        BIT                     NULL,
    CreatedBy                             INT                    NOT NULL,
    CreatedDate                            DATETIME2(3)           NOT NULL DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME()),
    ModifiedBy                              INT                    NULL,
    ModifiedDate                             DATETIME2(3)           NULL,
    IsActive                                   BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    IsDeleted                                    BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    RowVersion                                     ROWVERSION             NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT PK_ItemAttribute PRIMARY KEY CLUSTERED (ItemAttributeId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_ItemAttribute_Item FOREIGN KEY (ItemId) REFERENCES dbo.ItemMaster (ItemId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_ItemAttribute_Definition FOREIGN KEY (AttributeDefinitionId)
        REFERENCES dbo.AttributeDefinition (AttributeDefinitionId),
    CONSTRAINT UQ_ItemAttribute_ItemDefinition UNIQUE (ItemId, AttributeDefinitionId),
    CONSTRAINT CK_ItemAttribute_SingleValue CHECK (
        (CASE WHEN ValueText IS NOT NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) +
        (CASE WHEN ValueNumber IS NOT NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) +
        (CASE WHEN ValueDate IS NOT NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) +
        (CASE WHEN ValueBoolean IS NOT NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) = 1
    )
);
10.9.10 Worked Pattern Example: WarehouseItem (Chapter 6)
Demonstrating that the same translation pattern applies unchanged outside the core nine tables:
CREATE TABLE dbo.WarehouseItem (
    WarehouseItemId   BIGINT IDENTITY(1,1)   NOT NULL,
    ItemId             BIGINT                 NOT NULL,
    WarehouseId          INT                    NOT NULL,
    QuantityOnHand         DECIMAL(18,4)          NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    QuantityReserved         DECIMAL(18,4)          NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    BinLocation                 VARCHAR(30)            NULL,
    LastCountDate                 DATE                    NULL,
    CreatedBy                       INT                    NOT NULL,
    CreatedDate                       DATETIME2(3)           NOT NULL DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME()),
    ModifiedBy                          INT                    NULL,
    ModifiedDate                          DATETIME2(3)           NULL,
    IsActive                                BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (1),
    IsDeleted                                 BIT                    NOT NULL DEFAULT (0),
    RowVersion                                  ROWVERSION             NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT PK_WarehouseItem PRIMARY KEY CLUSTERED (WarehouseItemId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_WarehouseItem_Item FOREIGN KEY (ItemId) REFERENCES dbo.ItemMaster (ItemId),
    CONSTRAINT FK_WarehouseItem_Warehouse FOREIGN KEY (WarehouseId) REFERENCES dbo.Warehouse (WarehouseId),
    CONSTRAINT UQ_WarehouseItem_ItemWarehouse UNIQUE (ItemId, WarehouseId)
);
10.10 Chapter Summary
This chapter delivered the two artifacts a development team actually starts coding against: a versioned REST contract (Sections 10.1–10.8) covering the full create-approve-activate lifecycle, and executable T-SQL DDL (Section 10.9) for the nine core tables, with the translation pattern demonstrated once more against a supporting-chapter table to confirm it generalizes. Chapter 11 moves to the presentation layer: UI screen wireframes, developer flowcharts, and sequence diagrams for the same create-approve-activate flow shown here as JSON.
 
11. UI Screen Designs, Developer Flowcharts, Sequence Diagrams
This chapter is the presentation layer for the same create-approve-activate flow specified as JSON in Chapter 10: a visual entity-relationship diagram of the core schema, field-level wireframes for the three screens a developer will build first, a flowchart of the full item lifecycle, and sequence diagrams for the two most important request flows.
11.1 Entity-Relationship Diagram
Chapter 4 introduced the classification and attribute engine as a text-based ancestry diagram (Section 4.2). The rendered diagram below is the same nine tables, drawn as a conventional ERD with primary and foreign keys marked, for the developers and reviewers who need a picture rather than a tree of arrows.
 
Solid arrows are direct foreign keys (e.g. ItemGroup → ItemCategory). Dotted arrows are the denormalized ancestry columns on ItemMaster (Section 4.2's note). Dashed arrows are the AttributeTemplate relationship, which is assigned by default at the Family level and may be overridden per item. The equivalent diagrams for Chapter 5's supporting tables and Chapter 6's functional mappings follow the same box-and-arrow convention and are not repeated here, since their text-based ancestry diagrams (Sections 5.2–5.6 and 6.1–6.6) already convey the same relationships at a level of detail this chapter's diagram would only duplicate.
11.2 UI Screen Designs (Wireframes)
Field-level layout for the three screens a frontend developer builds first, matching the JSON contracts from Chapter 10 field-for-field.
11.2.1 Screen: Item Creation Form
Section	Field(s)	Behavior
Header	Item Name, Item Code (read-only, system-suggested per Section 3.8's numbering pattern)	Code field greys out once a Family is selected and stays editable only until first save
Classification	Category → Group → Sub-Group → Family, four cascading dropdowns	Each dropdown filters the next; selecting Family auto-loads its default AttributeTemplate
Attributes	Dynamic form section, one input per TemplateAttribute row (Section 4.9), rendered in DisplayOrder	Required attributes marked with an asterisk; form cannot submit for approval until all are filled
Flags	CanPurchase / CanSell / CanManufacture / CanStock / CanTransfer toggles	At least one of CanPurchase/CanSell/CanManufacture must be on before Save is enabled
Business Units	Multi-select list of BusinessUnit rows the item should be authorized for	Pre-checks the creating user's own unit; others require the BusinessUnitItem row on save
Footer	Save as Draft / Submit for Approval buttons	Submit is disabled until every required attribute and at least one business unit is set
11.2.2 Screen: Item Detail & Approval
Section	Field(s)	Behavior
Status Banner	ItemStatus badge, current approval step, VersionNumber	Color-coded: grey (Draft), amber (PendingApproval), green (Active), red (Rejected/Obsolete)
Approval Panel	Approve / Reject buttons, Comments field	Visible only to the user holding the current step's ApproverRole (Section 8.2); Comments required on Reject
Tabs	Details, Attributes, Business Entities, Documents, History	History tab lists ItemVersion and ItemAuditLog entries (Sections 8.3–8.4) in a single reverse-chronological feed
QC Panel (if applicable)	ItemQCProfile template name, pass/fail status per QCParameter	Only rendered when an ItemQCProfile row resolves for this item's family (Section 7.1.3)
11.2.3 Screen: Item Search & List
Section	Field(s)	Behavior
Filter Bar	Category, Status, Business Entity, free-text search	Maps directly to the query parameters in Section 10.5
Results Table	Item Code, Item Name, Category, Status, Business Entities (chip list)	Clicking a row opens Section 11.2.2's detail screen
Pagination	Page controls, page size selector (capped at 100)	Mirrors the pagination object in Section 10.5's response
11.3 Developer Flowchart: Item Lifecycle
The end-to-end path from a blank form to a transactable item, combining the state machine from Section 8.1 with the API calls from Chapter 10:
[ Item Creation Form ]  ── POST /items ──→  ItemStatus = Draft
        │
        │  fill required attributes, select business units
        ↓
POST /items/{id}/submit  ──→  ItemStatus = PendingApproval
        │
        ↓
[ ApprovalStep 1 ] ── Approved ──→ [ ApprovalStep 2 ] ── … ──→ [ Final Step ]
        │ Rejected                                              │ Approved
        ↓                                                        ↓
ItemStatus = Rejected ── back to Draft         ItemStatus = Active, VersionNumber = 1
                                                         │
                                          later edit ────────┤
                                                         ↓
                                          PATCH /items/{id} ──→ re-approval ──→ new ItemVersion
                                                         │
                                          Inactive ←───────┤───────→ Obsolete (+ ItemObsolescence)
11.4 Sequence Diagrams
11.4.1 Create Item → Submit → Approve
Client          API             ItemMaster tbl     ApprovalRequest tbl
  │  POST /items    │                  │                    │
  │──────────────→│                  │                    │
  │                 │── INSERT Draft ─→│                    │
  │←─ 201 Created ───┤                  │                    │
  │  POST /submit   │                  │                    │
  │──────────────→│                  │                    │
  │                 │── UPDATE PendingApproval →│              │
  │                 │────────────────────→ INSERT Request  │
  │←─ 202 Accepted ──┤                  │                    │
  │ POST /approval- │                  │                    │
  │ actions         │                  │                    │
  │──────────────→│                  │                    │
  │                 │────────────────────→ INSERT Action   │
  │                 │   (final step)   │                    │
  │                 │── UPDATE Active →│                    │
  │←─ 200 OK ─────┤                  │                    │

  │ GET /items?...  │                  │                    │

11.5 Chapter Summary
The visual ERD in Section 11.1, the three field-level wireframes in Section 11.2, the lifecycle flowchart in Section 11.3, and the two sequence diagrams in Section 11.4 give a frontend or full-stack developer everything needed to start building screens against the API contract from Chapter 10 without re-deriving the flow from the schema chapters. Chapter 12 turns to controls: the validation matrix, standard error messages, and security matrix that govern every one of these screens and endpoints.
 
12. Validation Matrix, Error Messages, Security Matrix
Every validation rule, error code, and access restriction in this document has so far been stated locally, inside the chapter that introduced the table it governs. That is correct for design rationale but useless as a QA checklist or a security review artifact — nobody reviewing access control wants to re-read eleven chapters to assemble the picture. This chapter consolidates all three into single reference tables.
12.1 Validation Matrix
Every rule below was introduced earlier in this document; this table exists purely so QA can test against one list rather than eleven chapters.
#	Table.Field	Rule	Enforced At	Ref.
1	ItemCategory.CategoryCode	2–10 chars, uppercase A–Z/0–9 only	DB + API	§4.3
2	ItemMaster.ItemCode	3–30 chars, unique, per-category numbering format, immutable post-transaction	DB + API	§4.10
3	ItemMaster (flags)	At least one of CanPurchase / CanSell / CanManufacture must be true	API	§4.10
4	ItemMaster.ItemStatus	Transitions must follow the state machine — no skipped states	API + trigger	§8.1
5	AttributeDefinition.DataType	Immutable once any ItemAttribute references it	Trigger	§4.7
6	ItemAttribute (value columns)	Exactly one of ValueText/Number/Date/Boolean populated, matching the definition's DataType	DB CHECK	§4.11
7	ItemMaster → Active	Every IsRequired TemplateAttribute must have a corresponding ItemAttribute row	API	§4.11, §8.2
8	ItemLot.QCStatus	Stock cannot be issued from a lot that is Pending, Failed, or Quarantined	API	§7.2.1
9	ItemLot.ExpiryDate	Issuance follows first-expired-first-out; an expired lot cannot be issued at all	API	§7.2.1, §9.6
10	ItemSerial.SerialNumber	Unique per ItemId	DB UQ	§7.2.2
11	ItemBarcode.BarcodeValue	Globally unique across all items	DB UQ	§7.3.1
12	ItemRFID.EPCCode	Globally unique across all tags	DB UQ	§7.3.2
13	BusinessUnit.CompanyId	Fixed FK to Company; structurally non-recursive, no cycle-check trigger needed	DB FK	§5.2, §9.1
14	BusinessUnitItem	Unique (ItemId, BusinessUnitId); item unusable at a unit with no active row	DB UQ + API	§5.3
15	GLMapping	Exactly one of ItemCategoryId, ItemId is non-null	DB CHECK	§6.4.1
16	TaxMapping	Unique per (target, Jurisdiction) — multiple jurisdictions expected, duplicates are not	DB UQ	§6.4.2, §9.2
17	BillOfMaterial.Status	At most one Active BOMVersion per ParentItemId at a time	API	§6.5.1
18	BOMComponent.ComponentItemId	Cannot equal its own BOM's ParentItemId; no circular BOM structure	Trigger	§6.5.2, §9.1
19	AssetMaster.AssetCode	Globally unique	DB UQ	§6.6.1
20	ItemMaster → Obsolete	Requires a matching ItemObsolescence row in the same transaction	API	§8.5
21	ItemVersion	Any field change to an Active item creates a new version row, never an in-place update	Trigger + API	§8.4
22	ItemApprovalAction	Actor must hold the ApproverRole assigned to the current step	API	§8.2, §12.3
23	SupplierItem.IsPreferred	At most one preferred supplier per item	Filtered UQ index	§6.2.2
24	WarehouseItem.QuantityOnHand	Never edited directly; only written by transaction posting	API	§6.1.2

12.3 Security Matrix
Role-based permissions across the actions this document has specified. ‘Scoped’ means the permission applies only within the user's own BusinessUnitId (Section 5.2–5.3), consistent with Chapter 9's cross-company visibility default-deny rule.
Action	Category Mgr	Finance Ctrl	QC Manager	Warehouse Clerk	Sales User	Read-Only

Edit Draft item	Yes (scoped)	No	No	No	No	No
Submit for approval	Yes (scoped)	No	No	No	No	No
Approve — Category step	Yes	No	No	No	No	No

A System Administrator role, omitted from the matrix above, implicitly holds every permission unscoped, plus user and role management itself — deliberately excluded from the table since it is the one role this document's access model does not attempt to constrain by business unit.
12.4 Chapter Summary
Twenty-four validation rules, fifteen error codes, and a role-by-action security matrix now exist in one place each, cross-referenced back to the chapter that justified every one of them. Chapter 13 closes the document with the migration strategy for bringing legacy item catalogues into this schema, and a final set of best-practice recommendations for the engineering team.
 
13. Migration Strategy & Best Practices
The preceding twelve chapters specify the target system. This chapter specifies how an existing, messier reality — spreadsheets, legacy ERP exports, and per-factory item lists with no shared coding standard — gets from where it is today into this schema, plus a closing set of principles for the engineering team to hold onto after this document stops being actively referenced line by line.
13.1 Guiding Principles for Migration
•	Migrate in dependency order: reference and configuration data first (categories, groups, sub-groups, families, UOM, business units, warehouses, suppliers), then ItemMaster and its attributes, then current-state balances (open stock, open lots, open serials) — never the reverse, since every later table's foreign keys depend on the earlier ones existing.
•	No migrated item reaches Active status by skipping the approval workflow (Chapter 8) — it reaches it through a dedicated Migration workflow template whose steps are satisfied by an automated data-quality gate (Section 13.4) rather than a human reviewer, but the ItemApprovalRequest/ItemApprovalAction/ItemVersion rows are still written, so a migrated item's history looks identical in kind to a manually created one, just with PerformedBy = a system/migration account.
•	A legacy item code that doesn't fit the new ItemCode numbering pattern (Section 3.8) is preserved as BusinessUnitItem.LocalItemCode (Section 5.3), not forced into the new format by truncation or invented characters.
•	Historical transactions are explicitly out of scope for migration, consistent with Section 1.2 — only current-state balances (Section 13.3) move across. A business that needs historical reporting continuity keeps read access to the legacy system rather than migrating its transaction history into this schema.
13.2 Legacy Data Assessment
Before any load script is written, each legacy source should be assessed against these questions:
Legacy Artifact	Assessment Question	Why It Matters
Free-text category field	Does it map cleanly to an existing Category/Group/Sub-Group/Family, or does a new one need to be configured first?	Prevents inventing classification values ad hoc mid-load (Chapter 3)
Item codes across multiple legacy systems	Are there items that are actually the same physical thing under two different legacy codes?	These are pre-emptive merge candidates (Section 9.6) — better resolved before go-live than after
Inconsistent or missing UOM	Does every legacy unit resolve to a UOM.UOMCode (Section 5.4), including obscure ones?	An item cannot migrate without a valid BaseUOMId
Supplier and price fields embedded in the item row	Can they be cleanly split into SupplierItem and SupplierPrice (Section 6.2)?	Prevents recreating the very design mistake this document exists to avoid
Missing audit history	Is there any created-by/created-date information at all?	If not, the migrated item's ItemVersion 1 is explicitly labeled a migrated baseline, not a reconstructed history
13.3 Field Mapping — Worked Example
A representative legacy spreadsheet row and its resolved destination across the schema:
Legacy Field	Legacy Value	Destination
ItemCode	OLD-LT-0091	BusinessUnitItem.LocalItemCode (new ItemMaster.ItemCode is system-generated per Section 3.8)
Description	Dell Laptop i7 32GB	ItemMaster.ItemName (cleaned/standardized), plus BRAND/MODEL/CPU/RAM ItemAttribute rows
Category	“IT Stuff” (free text)	Resolved to ItemFamily “Dell” under Category ICT Equipment → Computer → Laptop (Section 4.6)
UOM	“EA”	Resolved to UOM.UOMCode = PCS (Section 5.4)
Supplier	“Dell BD Distributor”	Resolved to Supplier row + a new SupplierItem mapping (Section 6.2.2)
Price	1200 (no currency stated)	SupplierPrice.UnitPrice = 1200, Currency defaulted to the group's base currency and flagged for confirmation
ReorderLevel	10	InventoryPolicy.ReorderLevel, scoped to the receiving BusinessUnitId (Section 6.1.3)
Warehouse / QtyOnHand	“HO Store” / 35	Warehouse resolved by name match, then one WarehouseItem row with QuantityOnHand = 35 (Section 6.1.2)
The blank currency on the legacy Price field is a common real-world gap, not a hypothetical — the migration process defaults it and flags the row for confirmation rather than blocking the whole batch or silently guessing wrong.
13.4 Migration Phases
Phase	Activity	Tables Populated
0	Discovery — inventory every legacy source per Section 13.2	—
1	Reference and configuration data load	Company, BusinessUnit, ItemCategory, ItemGroup, ItemSubGroup, ItemFamily, UOM, Warehouse, Supplier, AttributeDefinition, AttributeTemplate, TemplateAttribute
2	Item Master and attribute bulk load, landing in Draft	ItemMaster, ItemAttribute, BusinessUnitItem
3	Automated data-quality gate against the Chapter 12 validation matrix	— (read-only validation pass)
4	Migration approval — items passing Phase 3 move to Active via the Migration workflow template	ItemApprovalRequest, ItemApprovalAction, ItemVersion (version 1, labeled migrated baseline)
5	Current-state balances — open stock, open lots, open serials only	WarehouseItem, ItemLot, ItemSerial, InventoryPolicy
6	Parallel run — legacy and new system both live, reconciled daily	—
7	Cutover — legacy system frozen to read-only	—
8	Post-go-live stabilization (Section 13.6)	—
13.5 Cutover and Rollback
•	Parallel run duration: a minimum of one full operating cycle relevant to the slowest-moving business unit involved (e.g. a full harvest cycle for the Tea Estate, not just a calendar month), so seasonal items (Section 9.5) are exercised at least once before legacy is frozen.
•	Rollback trigger: if the daily reconciliation in Phase 6 shows a discrepancy rate above an agreed threshold for three consecutive days, cutover is deferred, not forced through on schedule.
•	Rollback mechanism: migrated data is never deleted on rollback — ItemMaster rows are set to Inactive (Section 8.1) and legacy remains the system of record until the discrepancy is root-caused and Phase 3–5 are re-run.
13.6 Post-Go-Live Stabilization
A defined hypercare period following cutover, during which the Section 12.2 error catalogue is monitored for rate, not just occurrence — a single ATTRIBUTE_REQUIRED_MISSING is normal user error; a spike in the same code from the same business unit signals a template or training gap, not a string of unrelated mistakes. Feedback from hypercare routinely results in AttributeTemplate and QCParameter adjustments (Sections 4.8, 7.1.2), which is expected and is why those tables were designed to be edited through configuration rather than a code release.
13.7 Best Practices — Closing Recap
Ten principles, each threaded through multiple chapters of this document, worth keeping on hand independent of the rest of the specification:
•	One item, one definition — never a duplicate ItemMaster row because two departments happen to use the same thing (Chapter 3).
•	Configuration over code — new categories, attribute templates, and QC templates are data, not deployments (Chapters 3–4, 7).
•	Never hard-delete — status changes and version rows, not DELETE statements, anywhere in this schema (Chapters 4, 8).
•	Separate master from transaction — ItemMaster defines what an item is; every other fact about it lives in a mapping or transaction table (Chapters 5–6).
•	Dynamic attributes over schema sprawl — a laptop and a bag of cement share a table; they do not share columns (Chapter 4).
•	Explicit mapping tables over boolean flags — BusinessUnitItem, not five unit-specific columns on ItemMaster (Chapter 5).
•	Version instead of overwrite — any question of “what did this look like on date X” has an exact answer (Chapter 8).
•	Approval before Active — no item becomes transactable by direct edit, human or migrated (Chapter 8, Chapter 13).
•	Global uniqueness with local aliasing — one ItemCode, with LocalItemCode absorbing every legacy naming inconsistency (Chapters 4–5, 13).
•	Category-first, item-level override — the same pattern reused for GLMapping, TaxMapping, and ItemQCProfile, so exceptions are handled without complicating the default case (Chapters 6–7).
