ColorMaster gained Space Grey (Dell Latitude's actual chassis color) proving the same table names fabric and electronics colors identically. C.7–C.8 updated accordingly.
13.9 Closing Statement
This document set out, across thirteen chapters, to prove a single claim made in Chapter 1: that fabric, laptops, cement, steel, hospital equipment, and software licenses can all be represented by the same core schema, differentiated by configuration rather than by code. Every worked example — the WeavoGo reference dataset, the ABC Group business unit expansion, the hospital medicine-and-MRI walkthrough — was chosen to stress that claim against a genuinely different domain, and in each case the same nine core tables from Chapter 4 held without modification. That is the test any future addition to this specification should be held to as well.
 
Appendix A: Human Capital Management (HCM) Master Data — Hire to Settlement
The people side of an enterprise has exactly the same design problem the item side had in Chapter 3: a garments ERP hard-codes piece-rate fields onto a staff table, a hospital HRIS hard-codes medical license numbers onto its own separate one, and neither can absorb the other without a schema fork. This appendix gives WeavoGo and TISWL one Human Capital Management master data foundation that covers a person's entire relationship with the organization — requisition, hire, employment, compensation, benefits, time off, performance, and eventual separation and final settlement — using the same architectural discipline as the Item Master: shallow classification, dynamic attributes, explicit mapping tables, and nothing hard-deleted.
This supersedes the earlier, lighter-weight version of this appendix. The single biggest correction is structural: a real HCM system never conflates “the person” with “the job.” A human being is hired, promoted, transferred, resigns, and is sometimes rehired years later — the biographical person is stable across all of that; the employment is not. Modeling both as one row, which the earlier Employee table did, breaks the moment someone leaves and returns. Section A.4 fixes that with a Person/Employee split, and everything downstream in this appendix is built on top of it.
As with Appendix A's earlier scope note: this is infrastructure the Item Master depends on for approvals and audit, but by the end of this appendix it is a peer module in its own right, not a thin supporting table. Consistent with Section 1.2, day-to-day transactions — a specific attendance punch, a specific leave application, a specific payroll run — remain out of scope; what follows is the master data those transactions would be posted against.
A.1 Organization Foundation
Company, BusinessUnit, Department, and CostCenter are specified in Section 5.2 and below; every table in this appendix ultimately anchors to a BusinessUnit, exactly as Warehouse and GLMapping do.
A.1.1 Table: Department
Primary Key: DepartmentId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: BusinessUnitId → BusinessUnit; ParentDepartmentId → Department.DepartmentId (nullable, self-referencing)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
DepartmentId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
BusinessUnitId	INT	No	—	The business unit this department belongs to
ParentDepartmentId	INT	Yes	NULL	Parent department, for sub-department roll-ups
DepartmentCode	VARCHAR(15)	No	—	Short code, unique within its unit
DepartmentName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display name
Constraints: UQ_Department_UnitCode — unique (BusinessUnitId, DepartmentCode); ParentDepartmentId requires the same cycle-detection trigger as BOMComponent's self-reference (Section 9.1).
Sample data:
Id	Unit Ref.	Code	Department Name
1	ICT & Software Division	NETOPS	Network Operations
2	ICT & Software Division	SECOPS	IT Security
3	Construction Division	PROCURE	Site Procurement
4	Hospital	PHARM	Pharmacy
5	Corporate Services	FIN	Finance & Accounts
6	Corporate Services	HR	Human Resources
A.1.2 Table: CostCenter
Primary Key: CostCenterId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: BusinessUnitId → BusinessUnit; DepartmentId → Department (nullable)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
CostCenterId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
BusinessUnitId	INT	No	—	Owning unit
DepartmentId	INT	Yes	NULL	Owning department, if department-scoped
CostCenterCode	VARCHAR(15)	No	—	Short code, unique within its unit
CostCenterName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display name
Sample data:
Id	Unit Ref.	Department Ref.	Code	Cost Center Name
1	Construction Division	Site Procurement	CC-CON-01	ABC Factory Building Project
2	Hospital	Pharmacy	CC-HOS-01	Central Pharmacy Operations
A.2 Job Architecture
Three levels, deliberately shallower than the item classification chain's five, because job structures don't carry the same combinatorial depth: a garment factory doesn't need a five-level breakdown to distinguish a sewing operator from a cutting master. This is the same judgment call Appendix A made before for Designation, now formalized as a proper hierarchy with grading and pay-band structure attached.
A.2.1 Table: JobFamily
Purpose: the broadest occupational grouping — the direct analogue of ItemCategory (Section 4.3).
Primary Key: JobFamilyId (INT IDENTITY)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
JobFamilyId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
FamilyCode	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Unique code, e.g. PRODUCTION, MEDICAL
FamilyName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display name
Sample data:
Id	Code	Job Family
1	PRODUCTION	Production
2	TECHNICAL	Technical
3	MEDICAL	Medical
4	ADMIN	Administrative
5	FIELD	Field / Agricultural
6	MANAGEMENT	Management
A.2.2 Table: JobGrade
Purpose: seniority/pay level within a job family — the direct analogue of ItemGroup (Section 4.4), and the level at which PayGrade (Section A.2.4) attaches.
Primary Key: JobGradeId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: JobFamilyId → JobFamily
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
JobGradeId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
JobFamilyId	INT	No	—	Parent job family
GradeCode	VARCHAR(15)	No	—	Short code, unique within its family
GradeName	VARCHAR(50)	No	—	Display name, e.g. Grade A, Senior, Entry
GradeLevel	INT	No	—	Numeric rank for sorting/comparison, 1 = most junior
Sample data:
Id	Family Ref.	Code	Grade Name	Level
1	Production	PRD-A	Grade A (Skilled)	3
2	Production	PRD-B	Grade B (Semi-Skilled)	2
3	Production	PRD-C	Grade C (Trainee)	1
4	Technical	TECH-SR	Senior	3
5	Technical	TECH-MID	Mid-Level	2
6	Field	FLD-STD	Standard	1
A.2.3 Table: Designation
Purpose: the job title itself — the direct analogue of ItemFamily (Section 4.6), including the same default-attribute-template assignment mechanism.
Primary Key: DesignationId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: JobGradeId → JobGrade; DefaultEmployeeAttributeTemplateId → EmployeeAttributeTemplate (nullable)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
DesignationId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
JobGradeId	INT	No	—	Parent job grade
DesignationCode	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Unique machine code, e.g. SEW-OP
DesignationName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display name, e.g. Sewing Machine Operator
DefaultEmployeeAttributeTemplateId	INT	Yes	NULL	Template new employees in this designation default to
Constraints: UQ_Designation_Code — unique (DesignationCode).
Sample data:
Id	Grade Ref.	Code	Designation	Default Template
1	Grade A (Skilled)	SEW-OP	Sewing Machine Operator	Sewing Operator
2	Grade A (Skilled)	CUT-MSTR	Cutting Master	Sewing Operator
3	Senior (Technical)	NET-ENG	Network Engineer	ICT Engineer
4	Senior (Technical)	IT-SEC	IT Security Analyst	ICT Engineer
5	— (Medical)	STAFF-NURSE	Staff Nurse	Nurse
6	Standard (Field)	TEA-PLUCK	Tea Plucker	Tea Plucker
7	Senior (Technical)	SITE-SUP	Site Supervisor	Generic Staff
8	— (Admin)	ACCOUNTANT	Accountant	Generic Staff
9	Standard (Field)	DRIVER	Driver	Driver
A.2.4 Table: PayGrade
Purpose: the salary band associated with a job grade, bounding what SalaryStructureTemplate (Section A.6.2) may assign.
Primary Key: PayGradeId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: JobGradeId → JobGrade
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
PayGradeId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
JobGradeId	INT	No	—	The job grade this band applies to
MinMonthlySalary	DECIMAL(18,2)	No	—	Band floor, in base currency
MaxMonthlySalary	DECIMAL(18,2)	No	—	Band ceiling, in base currency
Constraints: CK_PayGrade_Range — CHECK (MaxMonthlySalary >= MinMonthlySalary).
Sample data (amounts in BDT):
Id	Job Grade Ref.	Min Monthly Salary	Max Monthly Salary
1	Grade A (Skilled)	12,000	18,000
2	Grade B (Semi-Skilled)	9,000	13,000
3	Grade C (Trainee)	7,000	9,500
4	Senior (Technical)	45,000	70,000
5	Mid-Level (Technical)	30,000	45,000
6	Standard (Field)	8,000	12,000
A.3 The Person / Employee Separation
This is the structural correction the earlier version of this appendix needed. A Person row is created once per human being and never duplicated, regardless of how many times they join, leave, and rejoin the organization. An Employee row represents one specific employment stint — one contract, one designation, one reporting line — and a Person may have more than one Employee row across their lifetime, each with its own EmployeeCode.
A.3.1 Table: Person
Purpose: stable biographical data, independent of any specific job. Nationality is captured here rather than on Employee because it is a fact about the person, not about any one employment stint — and it is what LeavePolicy's NationalityCategory (Section A.7.11) checks against.
Primary Key: PersonId (INT IDENTITY)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
PersonId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
FullName	VARCHAR(150)	No	—	Legal name
DateOfBirth	DATE	No	—	Date of birth
Gender	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	As per national ID / self-declared
Nationality	VARCHAR(60)	No	—	Country of citizenship
NationalIdNumber	VARCHAR(30)	No	—	National ID / passport number
ContactPhone	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Primary contact number
ContactEmail	VARCHAR(150)	Yes	NULL	Personal email, distinct from any work UserAccount
PermanentAddress	VARCHAR(300)	Yes	NULL	Address of record
EmergencyContactName	VARCHAR(150)	Yes	NULL	Emergency contact
EmergencyContactPhone	VARCHAR(20)	Yes	NULL	Emergency contact number
Constraints: UQ_Person_NationalId — unique (NationalIdNumber).
Business rules: recruitment (Section A.5) creates a Candidate row first, not a Person row; Person is created exactly once, at the point of hire, and the Candidate record is linked to it — this is what prevents a rejected-then-later-hired candidate from producing two Person rows for the same human being. Whether a given Nationality counts as “Local” or “Foreign” for LeavePolicy purposes (Section A.7.11) is resolved by comparing this field against the group's country of operation, not stored as a separate flag — a group operating in more than one country would compare against whichever BusinessUnit's Company the employee's Employee row belongs to, rather than a single fixed value.
Sample data — the seven human beings behind every Employee row used throughout this appendix, plus an eighth, foreign-national example used in Section A.7.11's Earned Leave policy:
Id	Full Name	Date of Birth	Gender	Nationality	National ID	Contact Phone
1	Farhan Ahmed	1990-03-15	Male	Bangladesh	1990031501101	+8801711000101
2	Nusrat Jahan	1992-07-22	Female	Bangladesh	1992072201102	+8801711000102
3	Karim Hossain	1988-11-05	Male	Bangladesh	1988110501103	+8801711000103
4	Rehana Begum	1995-02-10	Female	Bangladesh	1995021001104	+8801711000104
5	Anita Chakma	1997-06-18	Female	Bangladesh	1997061801105	+8801711000105
6	Shirin Akter	1991-09-30	Female	Bangladesh	1991093001106	+8801711000106
7	Jahangir Alam	1985-01-25	Male	Bangladesh	1985012501107	+8801711000107
8	Michael Tan	1987-08-12	Male	Singapore	S8712345D	+65-9123-4567
A.3.2 Table: Employee
Purpose: one employment stint. This is the direct analogue of ItemMaster (Section 4.10) — the record every other HCM table in this appendix ultimately references.
Primary Key: EmployeeId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: PersonId → Person; DesignationId → Designation; DepartmentId → Department (nullable); BusinessUnitId → BusinessUnit; ReportingToEmployeeId → Employee.EmployeeId (nullable, self-referencing); EmployeeAttributeTemplateId → EmployeeAttributeTemplate (nullable override)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
EmployeeId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
PersonId	INT	No	—	The human being this employment belongs to
EmployeeCode	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Unique code for this specific employment stint
EmploymentSequenceNumber	INT	No	1	1 for a person's first stint, 2 for a rehire, and so on
DesignationId	INT	No	—	Job title / classification
DepartmentId	INT	Yes	NULL	Home department, where applicable
BusinessUnitId	INT	No	—	The unit this employment is on the payroll of
ReportingToEmployeeId	INT	Yes	NULL	Direct manager, for the org chart
EmploymentType	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Regular, Contract, DailyWage, PieceRate, or Seasonal
JoiningDate	DATE	No	—	First working day of this stint
ConfirmationDate	DATE	Yes	NULL	Date probation was confirmed, if applicable
EmployeeStatus	VARCHAR(20)	No	'Probation'	Probation, Active, OnLeave, Suspended, Separated
EmployeeAttributeTemplateId	INT	Yes	NULL	Overrides Designation's default template if set
Constraints: UQ_Employee_Code — unique (EmployeeCode); UQ_Employee_PersonSequence — unique (PersonId, EmploymentSequenceNumber); CK_Employee_Type — CHECK (EmploymentType IN ('Regular','Contract','DailyWage','PieceRate','Seasonal')); CK_Employee_Status — CHECK (EmployeeStatus IN ('Probation','Active','OnLeave','Suspended','Separated')).
Business rules:
•	A rehire creates a new Employee row against the same PersonId with EmploymentSequenceNumber incremented, never a reactivation of the old row — the old row's EmployeeStatus stays Separated and its full history (compensation, leave, performance) remains intact and untouched.
•	ReportingToEmployeeId is self-referencing and requires the same cycle-detection trigger as Department.ParentDepartmentId; unlike Company/BusinessUnit (Section 5.2, deliberately flattened), a reporting line is genuinely recursive.
•	EmployeeStatus cannot move to Active until every IsRequired EmployeeTemplateAttribute for the effective template has a corresponding EmployeeAttribute row (Section A.3.4) — the same gate Section 4.11 enforces for items reaching Active.
•	Not every Employee row has a corresponding UserAccount row (Section A.12.2) — having a payroll record and having system login access are independent facts, exactly as CanStock and CanSell are independent flags on ItemMaster.
Sample data — including one rehire (row 108) to demonstrate the Person/Employee split in practice:
Id	Person Ref.	Employee Code	Seq	Full Name	Designation Ref.	Status
101	1	WVG-EMP-0101	1	Farhan Ahmed	Network Engineer	Active
102	2	WVG-EMP-0102	1	Nusrat Jahan	IT Security Analyst	Active
103	3	WVG-EMP-0103	1	Karim Hossain	Accountant	Active
104	4	TISWL-EMP-0201	1	Rehana Begum	Sewing Machine Operator	Active
105	5	WVG-EMP-0202	1	Anita Chakma	Tea Plucker	Active
106	6	WVG-EMP-0203	1	Shirin Akter	Staff Nurse	Active
107	7	CON-EMP-0204	1	Jahangir Alam	Site Supervisor	Separated
108	7	CON-EMP-0301	2	Jahangir Alam	Site Supervisor	Active
109	8	CON-EMP-0401	1	Michael Tan	Site Supervisor	Active
Rows 107 and 108 are the same person (PersonId 7) — Jahangir Alam resigned as employment #1 (EmployeeCode CON-EMP-0204, now Separated) and was rehired eighteen months later as employment #2 (CON-EMP-0301, Active). His Person row, national ID, and biographical data were never re-entered; only a new Employee row was created.
A.3.3 Employee Dynamic Attribute Engine
Structurally identical to Section 4.7–4.9's item attribute engine, applied to employment records instead of items.
A.3.3.1 Table: EmployeeAttributeDefinition
Primary Key: EmployeeAttributeDefinitionId (INT IDENTITY)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
EmployeeAttributeDefinitionId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
AttributeCode	VARCHAR(50)	No	—	Unique machine code, e.g. MACHINE_TYPE
AttributeName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display label
DataType	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Text, Number, Boolean, Date, or Enum
UnitOfMeasure	VARCHAR(20)	Yes	NULL	e.g. BDT/piece, kg/day, years
Sample data:
Id	AttributeCode	AttributeName	DataType	UOM
1	MACHINE_TYPE	Machine Type	Text	—
2	SKILL_GRADE	Skill Grade	Text	—
3	PIECE_RATE	Piece Rate	Number	BDT/piece
4	MEDICAL_LICENSE_NO	Medical License No.	Text	—
5	LAND_PLOT_SECTION	Land Plot Section	Text	—
6	DAILY_QUOTA_KG	Daily Quota	Number	kg/day
7	LICENSE_CLASS	Driving License Class	Text	—
A.3.3.2 Table: EmployeeAttributeTemplate
Primary Key: EmployeeAttributeTemplateId (INT IDENTITY)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
EmployeeAttributeTemplateId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
TemplateCode	VARCHAR(30)	No	—	Unique machine code
TemplateName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display name
Sample data:
Id	TemplateCode	TemplateName
1	SEWING_OP	Sewing Operator
2	ICT_ENGINEER	ICT Engineer
3	NURSE	Nurse
4	TEA_PLUCKER	Tea Plucker
5	DRIVER	Driver
6	GENERIC_STAFF	Generic Staff
A.3.3.3 Table: EmployeeTemplateAttribute
Primary Key: EmployeeTemplateAttributeId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: EmployeeAttributeTemplateId → EmployeeAttributeTemplate; EmployeeAttributeDefinitionId → EmployeeAttributeDefinition
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
EmployeeTemplateAttributeId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
EmployeeAttributeTemplateId	INT	No	—	Parent template
EmployeeAttributeDefinitionId	INT	No	—	Attribute included
IsRequired	BIT	No	1	Must be filled before EmployeeStatus can be Active
Constraints: UQ_EmployeeTemplateAttribute_TemplateDefinition — unique (EmployeeAttributeTemplateId, EmployeeAttributeDefinitionId).
Sample data (Sewing Operator template, EmployeeAttributeTemplateId = 1, and Tea Plucker template, Id = 4):
Id	Template Ref.	Attribute Ref.	Required
1	Sewing Operator	Machine Type	Yes
2	Sewing Operator	Skill Grade	Yes
3	Sewing Operator	Piece Rate	Yes
4	Tea Plucker	Land Plot Section	Yes
5	Tea Plucker	Daily Quota	Yes
A.3.4 Table: EmployeeAttribute
Purpose: the actual values, per employment — identical in shape to ItemAttribute (Section 4.11).
Primary Key: EmployeeAttributeId (BIGINT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: EmployeeId → Employee; EmployeeAttributeDefinitionId → EmployeeAttributeDefinition
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
EmployeeAttributeId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
EmployeeId	INT	No	—	Employment this value belongs to
EmployeeAttributeDefinitionId	INT	No	—	Which attribute
ValueText	NVARCHAR(500)	Yes	NULL	Populated when DataType = 'Text' or 'Enum'
ValueNumber	DECIMAL(18,4)	Yes	NULL	Populated when DataType = 'Number'
ValueDate	DATE	Yes	NULL	Populated when DataType = 'Date'
ValueBoolean	BIT	Yes	NULL	Populated when DataType = 'Boolean'
Constraints: UQ_EmployeeAttribute_EmployeeDefinition — unique (EmployeeId, EmployeeAttributeDefinitionId); CK_EmployeeAttribute_SingleValue — identical single-non-null-column CHECK as ItemAttribute.
Sample data (Rehana Begum, EmployeeId 104):
Id	Attribute Ref.	ValueText	ValueNumber
1	Machine Type	Single Needle Lockstitch	—
2	Skill Grade	Grade A	—
3	Piece Rate	—	2.50 (BDT/piece)
A.4 Recruitment & Onboarding
The path from an approved headcount request to an Active Employee row, reusing Chapter 8's approval machinery rather than inventing a second one.
A.4.1 Table: JobRequisition
Purpose: an approved request to fill a position — the trigger for everything else in this section.
Primary Key: JobRequisitionId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: DesignationId → Designation; BusinessUnitId → BusinessUnit; DepartmentId → Department (nullable); RequestedBy → UserAccount
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
JobRequisitionId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
DesignationId	INT	No	—	Position being requested
BusinessUnitId	INT	No	—	Unit the position belongs to
DepartmentId	INT	Yes	NULL	Department, where applicable
HeadcountRequested	INT	No	1	Number of openings
Justification	VARCHAR(500)	Yes	NULL	Business reason for the request
RequisitionStatus	VARCHAR(20)	No	'Draft'	Draft, PendingApproval, Approved, Filled, or Closed
Business rules: RequisitionStatus follows the identical Draft → PendingApproval → Approved state discipline as ItemMaster (Section 8.1), through the same ApprovalWorkflowTemplate/ApprovalStep tables (Section 8.2) — a Requisition workflow template is simply another row in ApprovalWorkflowTemplate, not a new mechanism.
Sample data:
Id	Designation Ref.	Unit Ref.	Headcount	Status
1	Site Supervisor	Construction Division	1	Approved
2	Sewing Machine Operator	TISWL Unit 1	5	Approved
3	Network Engineer	ICT & Software Division	1	Filled
A.4.2 Table: Candidate
Purpose: a pre-hire person record, deliberately separate from Person (Section A.3.1) so that rejected or not-yet-hired candidates never pollute the biographical master.
Primary Key: CandidateId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: PersonId → Person (nullable — populated only once hired)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
CandidateId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
PersonId	INT	Yes	NULL	Linked once the candidate is hired; NULL until then
FullName	VARCHAR(150)	No	—	Candidate's name
ContactPhone	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Contact number
ContactEmail	VARCHAR(150)	Yes	NULL	Contact email
Source	VARCHAR(50)	Yes	NULL	e.g. Referral, Job Portal, Walk-in, Agency
AppliedDate	DATE	No	—	Date of first application
Business rules: on hire, a Person row is created (or, if the candidate was previously an employee, matched by NationalIdNumber) and CandidateId.PersonId is set — this is the one and only path by which a Person row comes into existence.
Sample data — row 1 is Jahangir Alam's rehire application, resolving to his existing PersonId rather than creating a duplicate:
Id	Person Ref.	Full Name	Source	Applied Date
1	7 (existing)	Jahangir Alam	Referral	2024-08-20
2	— (not hired)	Mizanur Rahman	Job Portal	2024-01-15
3	— (not hired)	Farida Yasmin	Walk-in	2024-02-20
A.4.3 Table: CandidateApplication
Purpose: links a Candidate to a specific JobRequisition and tracks pipeline stage.
Primary Key: CandidateApplicationId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: CandidateId → Candidate; JobRequisitionId → JobRequisition
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
CandidateApplicationId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
CandidateId	INT	No	—	The applicant
JobRequisitionId	INT	No	—	The position applied for
Stage	VARCHAR(20)	No	'Applied'	Applied, Screened, Interviewed, Offered, Hired, or Rejected
OfferedSalary	DECIMAL(18,2)	Yes	NULL	Proposed monthly salary, if an offer was extended
Constraints: UQ_CandidateApplication_CandidateRequisition — unique (CandidateId, JobRequisitionId).
Business rules: Stage = 'Hired' is the only trigger that creates the Employee row (Section A.3.2); every other stage transition leaves Candidate and CandidateApplication as the sole record of the process.
Sample data:
Id	Candidate Ref.	Requisition Ref.	Stage	Offered Salary
1	Jahangir Alam	Site Supervisor (Req. 1)	Hired	48,000
2	Mizanur Rahman	Network Engineer (Req. 3)	Rejected	—
3	Farida Yasmin	Sewing Machine Operator (Req. 2)	Interviewed	—
A.4.4 Table: OnboardingTask
Purpose: a standard checklist template — e.g. IT account provisioning, ID card issuance, policy acknowledgment — assignable by JobFamily or Designation.
Primary Key: OnboardingTaskId (INT IDENTITY)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
OnboardingTaskId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
TaskName	VARCHAR(150)	No	—	e.g. Issue ID Card, Provision Email Account
ResponsibleDepartmentId	INT	Yes	NULL	Department expected to complete it, e.g. IT or HR
Sample data:
Id	Task Name	Responsible Department
1	Issue ID Card	Human Resources
2	Provision Email Account	Network Operations
3	Policy Acknowledgment Signed	Human Resources
4	Safety Induction Training	Site Procurement
A.4.5 Table: EmployeeOnboarding
Purpose: tracks completion of onboarding tasks for a specific new Employee row.
Primary Key: EmployeeOnboardingId (BIGINT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: EmployeeId → Employee; OnboardingTaskId → OnboardingTask
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
EmployeeOnboardingId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
EmployeeId	INT	No	—	The new employee
OnboardingTaskId	INT	No	—	The checklist item
CompletedDate	DATE	Yes	NULL	NULL until completed
Sample data (Jahangir Alam's rehire, EmployeeId 108):
Id	Task Ref.	Completed Date
1	Issue ID Card	2024-10-02
2	Provision Email Account	2024-10-02
3	Policy Acknowledgment Signed	2024-10-03
A.5 Compensation Structure
Never a single Salary column on Employee — pay is built from named components, exactly as an item's price is never a column on ItemMaster (Section 6.2.3's rule, applied here to people).
A.5.1 Table: PayComponent
Primary Key: PayComponentId (INT IDENTITY)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
PayComponentId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ComponentCode	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Unique code, e.g. BASIC, HRA, PF_DEDUCT
ComponentName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display name
ComponentType	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Earning or Deduction
IsStatutory	BIT	No	0	1 if legally mandated (e.g. Provident Fund, income tax withholding)
Sample data:
Id	Code	Component	Type	Statutory
1	BASIC	Basic Salary	Earning	No
2	HRA	House Rent Allowance	Earning	No
3	MEDICAL	Medical Allowance	Earning	No
4	CONVEYANCE	Conveyance Allowance	Earning	No
5	OVERTIME	Overtime Pay	Earning	No
6	PIECE_PAY	Piece-Rate Pay	Earning	No
7	PF_DEDUCT	Provident Fund Deduction	Deduction	Yes
8	TAX_DEDUCT	Income Tax Withholding	Deduction	Yes
9	TIFFIN_BILL	Tiffin Bill	Earning	No
10	IFTAR_BILL	Iftar Bill	Earning	No
A.5.2 Table: SalaryStructureTemplate & SalaryStructureComponent
Purpose: a named bundle of pay components per pay grade — structurally identical to AttributeTemplate/TemplateAttribute (Section 4.8–4.9), applied to compensation.
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
SalaryStructureTemplateId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
TemplateCode	VARCHAR(30)	No	—	Unique code, e.g. PROD-GRADE-A
PayGradeId	INT	No	—	The pay grade this template is built for
SalaryStructureComponent (junction): SalaryStructureTemplateId → SalaryStructureTemplate, PayComponentId → PayComponent, CalculationBasis (FixedAmount or PercentOfBasic), DefaultValue.
Sample data (SalaryStructureTemplate):
Id	TemplateCode	PayGrade Ref.
1	PROD-GRADE-A	Grade A (Skilled), BDT 12,000–18,000
2	TECH-SENIOR	Senior (Technical), BDT 45,000–70,000
Sample data (SalaryStructureComponent, Template 2 — Senior Technical):
Component Ref.	Calculation Basis	Default Value
Basic Salary	FixedAmount	50,000
House Rent Allowance	PercentOfBasic	40%
Medical Allowance	FixedAmount	1,500
A.5.3 Table: EmployeeCompensation
Purpose: the actual, time-bound amount an employee is paid per component — structurally identical to SupplierPrice (Section 6.2.3): effective-dated, never overwritten in place.
Primary Key: EmployeeCompensationId (BIGINT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: EmployeeId → Employee; PayComponentId → PayComponent
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
EmployeeCompensationId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
EmployeeId	INT	No	—	Employment this pay component applies to
PayComponentId	INT	No	—	Which component
Amount	DECIMAL(18,2)	No	—	Amount for one payroll period
EffectiveFrom	DATE	No	—	First date this amount applies
EffectiveTo	DATE	Yes	NULL	Last date; NULL = still current
Business rules: a salary revision never updates Amount in place — it closes the prior row's EffectiveTo and inserts a new row, preserving exactly what was paid on any past date, the same discipline Chapter 6.2.3 applies to SupplierPrice.
Sample data — Rehana Begum (EmployeeId 104, piece-rate, no Basic row) and Farhan Ahmed (EmployeeId 101, Regular, full salary structure with a documented revision):
Id	Employee Ref.	Component Ref.	Amount	Effective From	Effective To
1	Rehana Begum	Piece-Rate Pay	2.50 / piece	2024-01-01	— (current)
2	Farhan Ahmed	Basic Salary	45,000	2023-01-01	2025-12-31
3	Farhan Ahmed	Basic Salary	50,000	2026-01-01	— (current)
4	Farhan Ahmed	House Rent Allowance	20,000	2026-01-01	— (current)
A.6 Benefits
A.6.1 Table: BenefitPlan

Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description

BenefitPlanId	INT	No	—	The plan enrolled in
EnrollmentDate	DATE	No	—	Date of enrollment

Sample data:

A.7 Time, Attendance & Leave Policy
Policy and entitlement master data only — individual attendance punches and leave applications are transactions, out of scope per Section 1.2, the same boundary drawn around WarehouseItem's stock ledger.
A.7.1 Table: ShiftMaster
Purpose: the shift definitions available anywhere in the group. A shift is ordinarily one contiguous block of time, but IsSplitShift marks the exception — a shift made of two or more separate work blocks with an unpaid gap between them, detailed in ShiftSegment (Section A.7.1.1). This is what a changeover shift needs: work a few hours, go off duty for an extended break, then return and work through to the following morning, all as a single shift record rather than two unrelated ones.
Primary Key: ShiftId (INT IDENTITY)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ShiftId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ShiftCode	VARCHAR(15)	No	—	Unique code, e.g. MORN, EVE, NIGHT, CHANGEOVER
ShiftName	VARCHAR(50)	No	—	Display name
StartTime	TIME	No	—	Overall start — for a split shift, the first segment's start (Section A.7.1.1)
EndTime	TIME	No	—	Overall end — for a split shift, the last segment's end
IsOvernight	BIT	No	0	1 if EndTime is on the following calendar day
IsSplitShift	BIT	No	0	1 if this shift has more than one ShiftSegment row; StartTime/EndTime then describe the envelope, not continuous work
BreakDurationMinutes	INT	No	0	Unpaid break within a single segment, e.g. lunch — distinct from the between-segment gap a split shift has
GraceMinutes	INT	No	10	Late-arrival grace period before a punch counts as late
Constraints: UQ_ShiftMaster_Code — unique (ShiftCode).
Business rules: IsOvernight = 1 shifts are attributed to the calendar date they start on, not the date they end on. For IsSplitShift = 1 shifts, actual worked/paid hours are the sum of each ShiftSegment's duration, not EndTime minus StartTime — the gap between segments is unpaid and unworked by definition.
Sample data — two shift sets in concurrent use across the group, plus the changeover shift used only on rotation-transition days (Section A.7.1.1):
Id	Code	Shift Name	Start	End	Overnight	Split	Grace
1	GEN	General Shift	08:00	17:00	No	No	10
2	NIGHT-2SET	Night Shift (2-Set)	20:00	05:00	Yes	No	15
3	MORN	Morning Shift	06:00	14:00	No	No	10
4	EVE	Evening Shift	14:00	22:00	No	No	10
5	NIGHT	Night Shift	22:00	06:00	Yes	No	15
6	FRI-HALF	Friday Half Shift	08:00	13:00	No	No	10
7	CHANGEOVER	Rotation Changeover Shift	08:00	08:00	Yes	Yes	10
A.7.1.1 Table: ShiftSegment
Purpose: breaks a split shift into its discrete work blocks. An ordinary shift needs no rows here at all — its single StartTime/EndTime on ShiftMaster is sufficient. Only IsSplitShift = 1 shifts, like the Changeover Shift, need this detail.
Foreign Keys: ShiftId → ShiftMaster
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ShiftSegmentId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ShiftId	INT	No	—	Parent shift
SegmentOrder	INT	No	—	1, 2, 3… — the order this block falls within the shift day
SegmentStartTime	TIME	No	—	This block's start
SegmentEndTime	TIME	No	—	This block's end
IsOvernightSegment	BIT	No	0	1 if SegmentEndTime is on the following calendar day
Constraints: UQ_ShiftSegment_ShiftOrder — unique (ShiftId, SegmentOrder).
Sample data (Changeover Shift, ShiftId 7 — work 08:00–14:00, an 8-hour unworked gap, then 22:00 through to 08:00 the next morning):
Id	Shift Ref.	Order	Start	End	Overnight
1	Changeover Shift	1	08:00	14:00	No
2	Changeover Shift	2	22:00	08:00	Yes
The 14:00–22:00 gap between the two segments is exactly where a normally-scheduled Evening Shift employee covers the floor — no special record is needed for that coverage, since it is simply that other employee's ordinary EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment for the day, resolved completely independently (Section A.7.9's worked example continues this scenario).
A.7.1.2 Table: ShiftGroup
Purpose: a named, ordered set of shifts that rotate together — the formal answer to “what shifts does this roster cycle through.” A roster references a ShiftGroup once (Section A.7.2) instead of hard-coding which shifts belong together into every rotating roster's pattern.
Primary Key: ShiftGroupId (INT IDENTITY)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ShiftGroupId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
GroupCode	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Unique code, e.g. 3SHIFT-SET
GroupName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display name
Sample data — the two shift sets named in this section's opening request:
Id	Code	Group Name
1	2SHIFT-SET	Two-Shift Set (General / Night)
2	3SHIFT-SET	Three-Shift Set (Morning / Evening / Night)
A.7.1.3 Table: ShiftGroupMember
Foreign Keys: ShiftGroupId → ShiftGroup; ShiftId → ShiftMaster
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ShiftGroupMemberId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ShiftGroupId	INT	No	—	Parent group
ShiftId	INT	No	—	Member shift
RotationOrder	INT	No	—	The order this shift is rotated into, 1, 2, 3…
Constraints: UQ_ShiftGroupMember_GroupOrder — unique (ShiftGroupId, RotationOrder); UQ_ShiftGroupMember_GroupShift — unique (ShiftGroupId, ShiftId).
Sample data:
Id	Group Ref.	Shift Ref.	Rotation Order
1	Two-Shift Set	General Shift	1
2	Two-Shift Set	Night Shift (2-Set)	2
3	Three-Shift Set	Morning Shift	1
4	Three-Shift Set	Evening Shift	2
5	Three-Shift Set	Night Shift	3
A.7.2 Table: ShiftRoster
Purpose: a named roster pattern. Two ways of driving it now coexist: a Rotating roster with ShiftGroupId set resolves automatically week by week from ShiftGroup/ShiftGroupMember (Section A.7.1.2–A.7.1.3) with no per-day data entry at all — the scalable path for the common case. ShiftRosterPattern (Section A.7.3) remains available underneath for a Fixed roster, or for a Rotating roster that needs a day-of-week pattern more irregular than “a whole week on one shift, then rotate.”
Primary Key: ShiftRosterId (INT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: ShiftGroupId → ShiftGroup (nullable); TransitionShiftId → ShiftMaster (nullable)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ShiftRosterId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
RosterCode	VARCHAR(30)	No	—	Unique code, e.g. TISWL-U1-3SHIFT
RosterName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display name
RosterType	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Fixed or Rotating
CycleLengthDays	INT	Yes	NULL	Only used when ShiftGroupId is NULL — the day-count cycle a manually-entered ShiftRosterPattern follows
ShiftGroupId	INT	Yes	NULL	The named shift set this roster automatically rotates through (Section A.7.1.2)
RotationUnit	VARCHAR(10)	Yes	NULL	Weekly, when ShiftGroupId is set — how often the group's shifts rotate
WeeksPerShift	INT	Yes	NULL	How many consecutive weeks an employee stays on each ShiftGroupMember before advancing to the next
WeeklyHolidayDayOfWeek	TINYINT	Yes	NULL	ISO day of week (1=Monday–7=Sunday) that is always off, regardless of rotation phase
TransitionShiftId	INT	Yes	NULL	The changeover shift (Section A.7.1) automatically used on the single day a rotation advances from one group member to the next
Constraints: UQ_ShiftRoster_Code — unique (RosterCode); CK_ShiftRoster_Type — CHECK (RosterType IN ('Fixed','Rotating')); CK_ShiftRoster_GroupOrPattern — CHECK (ShiftGroupId IS NOT NULL OR CycleLengthDays IS NOT NULL), ensuring every Rotating roster resolves one way or the other.
Business rules — automatic weekly resolution for a ShiftGroupId-driven roster:
•	WeeksElapsed = FLOOR((date − EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment.RosterStartDate) / 7); GroupPosition = FLOOR(WeeksElapsed / WeeksPerShift) MOD (number of ShiftGroupMember rows in the group); the resolved shift is the ShiftGroupMember row with RotationOrder = GroupPosition + 1 — the same anchor-date-plus-elapsed-time arithmetic as a day-cycling Rotating roster (Section A.7.3), now operating in whole weeks instead of days.
•	If date's ISO day of week equals WeeklyHolidayDayOfWeek, the employee is off regardless of GroupPosition.
•	On the single calendar day where GroupPosition is about to advance to the next rotation order — the day immediately following the outgoing week's last working day — TransitionShiftId is used instead of either the outgoing or incoming group member's shift, if TransitionShiftId is set. This is what puts an employee onto the Changeover Shift (Section A.7.1) exactly once per rotation, rather than cutting abruptly from one shift's end time to the next shift's start time.
Sample data — TISWL-U1-3SHIFT now driven entirely by ShiftGroup rather than 21 manually-entered pattern rows, and a Fixed roster shown for contrast, which still relies on ShiftRosterPattern (Section A.7.3):
Id	Code	Type	Shift Group Ref.	Weeks/Shift	Holiday DOW	Transition Shift Ref.
1	TISWL-U1-3SHIFT	Rotating	Three-Shift Set	1	5 (Friday)	Changeover Shift
2	WVG-ICT-FIXED	Fixed	— (uses A.7.3 pattern)	—	—	—
3	WVG-HOS-3SHIFT	Rotating	Three-Shift Set	1	5 (Friday)	Changeover Shift
TISWL-U1-3SHIFT no longer needs the 21-row ShiftRosterPattern table Section A.7.3 previously required to represent this exact rotation — six columns on this one row now derive the identical schedule automatically. ShiftRosterPattern is retained for rosters like WVG-ICT-FIXED, and for any Rotating roster whose pattern genuinely cannot be expressed as “a whole week per shift.”
A.7.3 Table: ShiftRosterPattern
Purpose: the day-by-day (Fixed) or cycle-day-by-cycle-day (custom Rotating) shift assignment within a roster, effective-dated so a pattern change never edits history in place. Since Section A.7.2 added automatic weekly resolution for the common “a whole week per shift” case, this table is now needed for two narrower purposes: Fixed rosters (which always resolve by day of week, never by group rotation), and any Rotating roster whose pattern is irregular enough that group-based weekly resolution cannot express it.
Foreign Keys: ShiftRosterId → ShiftRoster; ShiftId → ShiftMaster (nullable — NULL means a day off)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ShiftRosterPatternId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ShiftRosterId	INT	No	—	Parent roster
SequenceDay	INT	No	—	For Fixed: ISO day of week, 1=Monday–7=Sunday. For a custom Rotating pattern: cycle day, 1..CycleLengthDays
ShiftId	INT	Yes	NULL	The shift worked on this day; NULL if this is a day off
IsWeeklyHoliday	BIT	No	0	Marks this day as the standing weekly holiday, distinct from an ad hoc day off
EffectiveFrom	DATE	No	—	First date this pattern row applies
EffectiveTo	DATE	Yes	NULL	Last date; NULL = still current
Constraints: UQ_ShiftRosterPattern_RosterDayEffective — unique (ShiftRosterId, SequenceDay, EffectiveFrom); CK_ShiftRosterPattern_SequenceDay — CHECK (SequenceDay BETWEEN 1 AND 366).
Business rules:
•	For a Fixed roster, the shift on any calendar date is looked up directly by that date's ISO day of week — no anchor date is needed.
•	For a custom Rotating roster with no ShiftGroupId (Section A.7.2), the shift on any calendar date is computed as SequenceDay = ((date − EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment.RosterStartDate) MOD CycleLengthDays) + 1, then looked up against whichever pattern row's [EffectiveFrom, EffectiveTo) window contains that date.
•	When CycleLengthDays is a multiple of 7, anchoring RosterStartDate to the desired weekly-holiday weekday keeps that day off landing on the same weekday every cycle, without hard-coding it anywhere in the schema.
•	Changing a roster's pattern closes the affected rows' EffectiveTo and inserts new rows with a later EffectiveFrom, rather than updating SequenceDay/ShiftId in place; a historical attendance or payroll calculation for a past date always resolves against the pattern that was actually in force on that date.
Sample data (WVG-ICT-FIXED, RosterId 2 — a standard fixed week with Friday off; TISWL-U1-3SHIFT no longer needs a row here at all, since Section A.7.2's ShiftGroup-driven columns resolve it automatically):
SequenceDay	Day	Shift Ref.	Weekly Holiday	Effective From
1	Monday	General Shift	No	2023-01-01
2	Tuesday	General Shift	No	2023-01-01
3	Wednesday	General Shift	No	2023-01-01
4	Thursday	General Shift	No	2023-01-01
5	Friday	— (day off)	Yes	2023-01-01
6	Saturday	General Shift	No	2023-01-01
7	Sunday	General Shift	No	2023-01-01
A.7.4 Table: EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment
Purpose: assigns an employee to a roster, replacing the earlier version of this appendix's flat EmployeeShiftAssignment — the shift an employee works on any given day is now derived from this assignment plus ShiftRosterPattern, not stored redundantly per day.
Foreign Keys: EmployeeId → Employee; ShiftRosterId → ShiftRoster
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
EmployeeShiftRosterAssignmentId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
EmployeeId	INT	No	—	The employee assigned
ShiftRosterId	INT	No	—	The roster assigned
RosterStartDate	DATE	No	—	Anchor date for cycle-day-1, used only when the roster is Rotating (Section A.7.3)
EffectiveFrom	DATE	No	—	Start of this assignment
EffectiveTo	DATE	Yes	NULL	NULL = current
Constraints: an employee may hold at most one EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment row with EffectiveTo IS NULL at a time — enforced by a filtered unique index on EmployeeId, the same pattern SupplierItem.IsPreferred uses (Section 6.2.2).
Sample data:
Id	Employee Ref.	Roster Ref.	Roster Start Date	Effective From
1	Rehana Begum	TISWL Unit 1 Rotating 3-Shift	2026-01-03 (a Saturday)	2026-01-02
2	Farhan Ahmed	WeavoGo ICT Standard Weekly	— (Fixed roster)	2023-01-01
A.7.5 Table: EmployeeShiftOverride
Purpose: a single-date exception to whatever EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment and ShiftRosterPattern would otherwise resolve — a call-in on a scheduled day off, a shift swap between two colleagues, temporary coverage for someone on leave. Without this table, any of those cases would force editing the roster pattern itself for a single person on a single day, which is exactly the kind of one-off edit Section A.7.3's effective-dating discipline exists to avoid.
Foreign Keys: EmployeeId → Employee; OverrideShiftId → ShiftMaster (nullable — NULL forces a day off); ApprovedBy → UserAccount
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
EmployeeShiftOverrideId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
EmployeeId	INT	No	—	The employee affected
OverrideDate	DATE	No	—	The single calendar date this override applies to
OverrideShiftId	INT	Yes	NULL	The shift actually worked; NULL if the employee is being forced off despite the roster scheduling them to work
OverrideType	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	CallIn, Swap, Coverage, or ForcedOff
Reason	VARCHAR(300)	Yes	NULL	Free-text justification
ApprovedBy	INT	No	—	The approving manager
Constraints: UQ_EmployeeShiftOverride_EmployeeDate — unique (EmployeeId, OverrideDate); CK_EmployeeShiftOverride_Type — CHECK (OverrideType IN ('CallIn','Swap','Coverage','ForcedOff')).
Business rules:
•	Resolution order for “what shift does this employee work on this date” is: EmployeeShiftOverride first, if a row exists for that EmployeeId/OverrideDate; otherwise fall through to EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment + ShiftRosterPattern (Sections A.7.3–A.7.4). An override never modifies the underlying roster or pattern rows.
•	ShiftAllowance (Section A.7.6) and OvertimePolicy's DayType resolution (Section A.7.8) both key off whichever shift and day-type actually apply after this override is taken into account — an EmployeeShiftOverride with OverrideType = 'CallIn' on a day ShiftRosterPattern.IsWeeklyHoliday = 1 is exactly what makes that day's overtime resolve to the WeeklyHoliday rate rather than Regular (Section A.7.9's worked example).
•	A Swap override is recorded as two rows — one per employee — rather than a single row referencing both, keeping the table's grain at “one employee, one date” consistent with every other row in it.
Sample data — the emergency call-in from Section A.7.9's worked example, now backed by an actual row instead of only narrated:
Id	Employee Ref.	Override Date	Shift Ref.	Type	Reason
1	Rehana Begum	2026-01-23	Night Shift	CallIn	Emergency shipment coverage
A.7.6 Table: ShiftAllowance
Purpose: the standard daily allowance — a Tiffin Bill, for instance — earned automatically whenever an employee's roster resolves to a given shift on a working day, without a separate EmployeeCompensation row (Section A.5.3) being maintained per employee for it. BusinessUnitId is optional, following the same default-plus-override pattern as GLMapping and TaxMapping (Section 6.4): a NULL BusinessUnitId row is the group-wide default rate for that shift, and a specific BusinessUnitId row overrides it for one unit wherever local pay scales differ.
Foreign Keys: ShiftId → ShiftMaster; PayComponentId → PayComponent; BusinessUnitId → BusinessUnit (nullable)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
ShiftAllowanceId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
ShiftId	INT	No	—	The shift this allowance applies to
PayComponentId	INT	No	—	Which pay component it is paid as
BusinessUnitId	INT	Yes	NULL	NULL = group-wide default rate; set = override for this specific unit
Amount	DECIMAL(18,2)	No	—	Amount per working day on this shift
EffectiveFrom	DATE	No	—	First date this rate applies
EffectiveTo	DATE	Yes	NULL	NULL = current
Constraints: UQ_ShiftAllowance_ShiftUnitEffective — unique (ShiftId, BusinessUnitId, EffectiveFrom); the pair (ShiftId, NULL) may only have one currently-effective row, and likewise for each (ShiftId, a specific BusinessUnitId).
Business rules: resolution for a given employee always checks for a BusinessUnitId-specific row matching their EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment's unit first; if none exists, it falls back to the NULL-BusinessUnitId default row for that shift — the same lookup order GLMapping and TaxMapping use for category-versus-item targeting (Section 6.4), applied here to unit-versus-group targeting instead. Like EmployeeCompensation (Section A.5.3) and SupplierPrice (Section 6.2.3), a rate change never overwrites Amount in place.
Sample data — the group-wide default rates, plus a TISWL-specific override showing Night Shift paid higher at that unit than the group default:
Id	Shift Ref.	Component Ref.	Unit Override	Amount	Effective From
1	General Shift	Tiffin Bill	— (group default)	50.00	2026-01-01
2	Evening Shift	Tiffin Bill	— (group default)	80.00	2026-01-01
3	Night Shift	Tiffin Bill	— (group default)	100.00	2026-01-01
4	Night Shift	Tiffin Bill	TISWL Unit 1	130.00	2026-01-01
Row 4 overrides row 3 specifically for TISWL Unit 1 — an employee on Night Shift at TISWL Unit 1 (such as Rehana Begum) resolves to 130.00, while a Night Shift employee at any other unit resolves to the 100.00 group default. Adding a fifth unit-specific rate tomorrow requires one more row, not a change to this table's shape.
A.7.7 Table: SpecialPeriod, SpecialPeriodAllowance & SpecialPeriodShiftOverride
Purpose: everything bound to a calendar window rather than a permanent policy — an Iftar Bill during Ramadan, and Ramadan's shortened working hours, are both modeled here, general enough to also cover a winter allowance or a monsoon transport allowance without a new table per occasion.
Primary Key (SpecialPeriod): SpecialPeriodId (INT IDENTITY)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
SpecialPeriodId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
PeriodCode	VARCHAR(30)	No	—	Unique code, e.g. RAMADAN_2026
PeriodName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display name
StartDate	DATE	No	—	First date of the period
EndDate	DATE	No	—	Last date of the period
PeriodType	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Religious, Seasonal, or Other
Sample data (SpecialPeriod):
Id	Code	Period Name	Start Date	End Date	Type
1	RAMADAN_2026	Ramadan 1447H	2026-02-18	2026-03-19	Religious
SpecialPeriodAllowance (junction): SpecialPeriodId → SpecialPeriod, PayComponentId → PayComponent, ShiftId → ShiftMaster (nullable — NULL applies regardless of shift), Amount.
Business rules: applies only to an employee whose EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment resolves them to the specified ShiftId (Section A.7.4, via A.7.1–A.7.2) on a date within [StartDate, EndDate]; a NULL ShiftId row applies to every working employee in the period regardless of shift. Because IFTAR_BILL and TIFFIN_BILL are distinct PayComponent codes, the two stack by default — a factory wanting Iftar to replace rather than add to Tiffin during Ramadan would simply omit the ShiftAllowance row for that shift during the period instead of introducing a special case here.
Sample data (SpecialPeriodAllowance) — Iftar Bill paid to Evening and Night shift workers, whose shifts span the sunset iftar time during Ramadan:
Id	Period Ref.	Component Ref.	Shift Ref.	Amount
1	Ramadan 1447H	Iftar Bill	Evening Shift	120.00
2	Ramadan 1447H	Iftar Bill	Night Shift	120.00
SpecialPeriodShiftOverride: purpose — shift timing itself often changes during Ramadan (shortened working hours are standard practice in many jurisdictions), which is a different kind of override than an allowance: the clock times change, not just the pay. This table lets a shift's StartTime/EndTime (Section A.7.1) be temporarily replaced for the duration of a SpecialPeriod, without touching ShiftMaster itself or creating a second, Ramadan-only copy of every shift.
Foreign Keys: SpecialPeriodId → SpecialPeriod; ShiftId → ShiftMaster
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
SpecialPeriodShiftOverrideId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
SpecialPeriodId	INT	No	—	The bounded period this override applies during
ShiftId	INT	No	—	The shift whose timing is temporarily replaced
OverrideStartTime	TIME	No	—	Start time in effect during the period
OverrideEndTime	TIME	No	—	End time in effect during the period
OverrideIsOvernight	BIT	No	0	1 if OverrideEndTime is on the following calendar day
Constraints: UQ_SpecialPeriodShiftOverride_PeriodShift — unique (SpecialPeriodId, ShiftId).
Business rules:
•	For any date within [SpecialPeriod.StartDate, EndDate], if a SpecialPeriodShiftOverride row exists for the shift an employee's roster resolves to that day, OverrideStartTime/EndTime are used for attendance, grace-period, and overtime-threshold purposes in place of ShiftMaster's own StartTime/EndTime. Everything else about the shift — its ShiftCode, its position in a ShiftGroup rotation (Section A.7.1.2–A.7.1.3), its ShiftAllowance rate (Section A.7.6) — is unaffected; only the clock times change.
•	A shift with no SpecialPeriodShiftOverride row for a given SpecialPeriod simply keeps its normal ShiftMaster timing during that period — not every shift needs shortened Ramadan hours (a corporate day-shift office role commonly does; a continuous-process Night Shift covering plant machinery commonly does not), and this table lets that decision be made shift by shift rather than site-wide.
Sample data — General and Morning Shift both shortened by roughly two hours during Ramadan 1447H; Night Shift left unchanged:
Id	Period Ref.	Shift Ref.	Normal Timing	Ramadan Timing
1	Ramadan 1447H	General Shift	08:00–17:00	09:00–15:00
2	Ramadan 1447H	Morning Shift	06:00–14:00	06:00–12:00
Night Shift deliberately has no override row here — continuous plant operations at TISWL Unit 1 keep running through Ramadan on their normal 22:00–06:00 timing, while the General and Morning Shift roles shown above end early enough for employees to prepare for iftar at home. Both decisions live in the same table, distinguished only by which shifts have a row and which don't.
A.7.8 Table: OvertimePolicy
Purpose: the rate multiplier applied to overtime hours, varying by employment type and by what kind of day the overtime falls on — overtime on the standing weekly holiday is paid at a different rate than overtime on an ordinary working day. Actual overtime hours worked on a given date are attendance transactions, out of scope per Section 1.2; this table supplies only the rate that a payroll calculation multiplies those hours by.
Primary Key: OvertimePolicyId (INT IDENTITY)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
OvertimePolicyId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
PolicyName	VARCHAR(100)	No	—	Display name
EmploymentType	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Regular, Contract, DailyWage, PieceRate, or Seasonal
DayType	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Regular, WeeklyHoliday, or PublicHoliday
ThresholdMinutesPerDay	INT	No	0	Minutes beyond the shift's scheduled duration before overtime begins
RateMultiplier	DECIMAL(4,2)	No	—	Multiplier applied to the employee's standard hourly-equivalent rate
Constraints: CK_OvertimePolicy_DayType — CHECK (DayType IN ('Regular','WeeklyHoliday','PublicHoliday')); UQ_OvertimePolicy_TypeDay — unique (EmploymentType, DayType).
Business rules: DayType = 'WeeklyHoliday' is resolved from the employee's ShiftRosterPattern.IsWeeklyHoliday (Section A.7.3) for that date; DayType = 'PublicHoliday' is resolved against a public holiday calendar, itself out of scope for the same reason attendance transactions are.
Sample data:
Id	Policy Name	Employment Type	Day Type	Multiplier
1	Standard Weekday OT	Regular	Regular	1.5x
2	Weekly Holiday OT	Regular	WeeklyHoliday	2.0x
3	Public Holiday OT	Regular	PublicHoliday	2.0x
4	Piece-Rate Weekday OT	PieceRate	Regular	1.5x
A.7.9 Worked Example: An Automatically-Resolved Rotation, a Changeover Shift, and a Holiday Overtime Shift
Rehana Begum (Sewing Machine Operator, TISWL Unit 1, EmployeeId 104) traced through every table added in this section:
•	She is assigned the TISWL-U1-3SHIFT rotating roster (ShiftRoster 1) via EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment, anchored at RosterStartDate = 2026-01-03, a Saturday. No ShiftRosterPattern rows exist for this roster at all — ShiftRoster 1's ShiftGroupId (Three-Shift Set), WeeksPerShift = 1, and WeeklyHolidayDayOfWeek = 5 (Friday) are the only configuration needed (Section A.7.2).
•	Week 1 (2026-01-03 to 2026-01-09) resolves to GroupPosition 0 → Morning Shift on the six working days, with Friday 2026-01-09 off. Week 2 (2026-01-10 to 2026-01-16) resolves to GroupPosition 1 → Evening Shift, with Friday 2026-01-16 off — both computed automatically from the same six roster columns, not read from any per-day table.
•	2026-01-17, the first day of Week 3, is the day GroupPosition advances from Evening (1) to Night (2). Because ShiftRoster 1's TransitionShiftId is set to the Changeover Shift, Rehana works that shift instead of jumping straight from Evening's 14:00–22:00 into Night's 22:00–06:00: her ShiftSegment rows (Section A.7.1.1) put her on the floor 08:00–14:00, off duty 14:00–22:00, then back on at 22:00 through to 08:00 the next morning. During her 14:00–22:00 gap, coverage comes from whichever colleague's own roster resolves to Evening Shift that same day — no override or special record is needed for that coverage, since it is simply that other employee's completely ordinary EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment for 2026-01-17.
•	From 2026-01-18 onward she is on Night Shift proper. ShiftAllowance pays her Tiffin Bill at 130.00/day — not the 100.00 group default (Section A.7.6), but TISWL Unit 1's own override rate for Night Shift, resolved automatically ahead of the default.
•	That week falls inside Ramadan 1447H (2026-02-18–2026-03-19)— in a later cycle of the same rotation, once her Night Shift week recurs during Ramadan, SpecialPeriodAllowance's row for Night Shift during this SpecialPeriod additionally pays her Iftar Bill at 120.00/day, stacking on top of her Tiffin Bill exactly as Section A.7.7 describes.
•	On Friday 2026-01-23 — her weekly holiday, per WeeklyHolidayDayOfWeek — she is called in for an emergency shipment and works a 4-hour shift, recorded as an EmployeeShiftOverride row (Section A.7.5, OverrideType = 'CallIn') rather than any edit to her roster. Because that date is her WeeklyHolidayDayOfWeek, OvertimePolicy resolves DayType = 'WeeklyHoliday' rather than 'Regular', and her overtime is paid at the 2.0x multiplier (Section A.7.8, policy row 2) instead of the standard 1.5x.
No new table was needed to combine an automatically-resolved weekly rotation, a mid-rotation changeover shift with built-in coverage by an unrelated employee's ordinary schedule, a unit-specific allowance override, an ad hoc call-in, a Ramadan-only allowance, and holiday overtime pay for the same employee — eleven tables (ShiftMaster, ShiftSegment, ShiftGroup, ShiftGroupMember, ShiftRoster, ShiftRosterPattern, EmployeeShiftRosterAssignment, EmployeeShiftOverride, ShiftAllowance, SpecialPeriod/SpecialPeriodAllowance, OvertimePolicy) resolve all six concerns independently, the same configuration-over-code discipline this document has held to since Chapter 3.
A.7.10 Table: LeaveType
Primary Key: LeaveTypeId (INT IDENTITY)
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
LeaveTypeId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
LeaveCode	VARCHAR(15)	No	—	Unique code, e.g. CL, SL, EL, MAT
LeaveName	VARCHAR(50)	No	—	Display name
IsPaid	BIT	No	1	Whether this leave type is paid
AccrualBasis	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	CalendarYear or ServiceYear — determines how EmployeeLeaveBalance's leave-year window is computed (Section A.7.11)
Constraints: CK_LeaveType_AccrualBasis — CHECK (AccrualBasis IN ('CalendarYear','ServiceYear')).
Sample data — Earned Leave is deliberately ServiceYear while the others remain CalendarYear, reflecting standard practice of anchoring earned/privilege leave to an employee's date of joining rather than the fiscal calendar:
Id	Code	Leave Type	Paid	Accrual Basis
1	CL	Casual Leave	Yes	CalendarYear
2	SL	Sick Leave	Yes	CalendarYear
3	EL	Earned Leave	Yes	ServiceYear
4	MAT	Maternity Leave	Yes	CalendarYear
5	LWP	Leave Without Pay	No	CalendarYear
A.7.11 Table: LeavePolicy
Purpose: annual entitlement per leave type, now scoped by both EmploymentType and NationalityCategory, since a foreign national's Earned Leave entitlement is commonly set higher than a local employee's under the same designation — and, independently, how much of it may ever be carried forward or paid out is set per policy row too, not assumed uniform across every employee the policy covers.
Foreign Keys: LeaveTypeId → LeaveType
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
LeavePolicyId	INT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
LeaveTypeId	INT	No	—	The leave type this policy governs
EmploymentType	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	Regular, Contract, DailyWage, PieceRate, or Seasonal
NationalityCategory	VARCHAR(10)	No	'Any'	Local, Foreign, or Any — Any applies regardless of Person.Nationality (Section A.3.1)
AnnualEntitlementDays	DECIMAL(5,2)	No	—	Days accrued per leave-year window
MaxCarryForwardDays	DECIMAL(5,2)	Yes	NULL	Cap on unused days carried into the next window; NULL = unlimited, 0 = none carried
IsEncashable	BIT	No	0	Whether unused days beyond the carry-forward cap may be paid out rather than lapsing
MaxEncashableDays	DECIMAL(5,2)	Yes	NULL	Hard ceiling on days payable in any single encashment event, in-service (Section A.7.13) or at final settlement (Section A.10.4); NULL = no ceiling beyond whatever is actually being encashed
Constraints: UQ_LeavePolicy_TypeEmploymentNationality — unique (LeaveTypeId, EmploymentType, NationalityCategory); CK_LeavePolicy_NationalityCategory — CHECK (NationalityCategory IN ('Local','Foreign','Any')).
Business rules: a lookup for a specific employee prefers an exact (EmploymentType, NationalityCategory) match over an 'Any' row on either dimension — the same specific-beats-general precedence GLMapping and TaxMapping use for category-versus-item targeting (Section 6.4), applied here across two scoping dimensions instead of one.
Sample data — rows 6 and 7 are both foreign-national Earned Leave, at the same 30-day entitlement, but with opposite carry-forward treatment: a Regular foreign employee carries unused days forward with no cap, while a Contract foreign employee carries none forward at all, everything above zero being encashed instead, capped at 20 days per encashment event regardless of how large the actual excess is:
Id	Leave Type Ref.	Employment Type	Nationality	Annual Days	Max Carry Fwd.	Encashable	Max Encash.
1	Casual Leave	Regular	Any	10	0 (lapses)	No	—
2	Casual Leave	PieceRate	Any	7	0 (lapses)	No	—
3	Sick Leave	Regular	Any	14	0 (lapses)	No	—
4	Earned Leave	Regular	Local	18	15	Yes	20
5	Earned Leave	DailyWage	Any	0	—	No	—
6	Earned Leave	Regular	Foreign	30	— (unlimited)	Yes	20
7	Earned Leave	Contract	Foreign	30	0 (none carried)	Yes	20
Rows 6 and 7 answer exactly the two cases described when this policy was scoped: a foreign national is entitled to 30 days regardless of contract type, but whether those unused days are carried to the next service year or not is a separate, independently configurable decision — “carried to next if available” for one employment type, “not carried forward” for another, both expressed as ordinary rows in this table rather than as exceptions to it.
A.7.12 Table: EmployeeLeaveBalance
Purpose: the cached ledger for one employee, one leave type, one leave-year window — now broken into opening balance, accrual, consumption, and encashment rather than a single DaysAvailable figure, so that a carried-forward balance is traceable back to the window it came from rather than appearing from nowhere. The window itself follows LeaveType.AccrualBasis (Section A.7.10): CalendarYear types use January 1–December 31, while ServiceYear types (Earned Leave) use a window anchored to Employee.JoiningDate, rolling forward on each anniversary rather than resetting on January 1.
Foreign Keys: EmployeeId → Employee; LeaveTypeId → LeaveType
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
EmployeeLeaveBalanceId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
EmployeeId	INT	No	—	The employee
LeaveTypeId	INT	No	—	The leave type
LeaveYearStartDate	DATE	No	—	First date of this balance's leave-year window
LeaveYearEndDate	DATE	No	—	Last date of this balance's leave-year window
OpeningBalance	DECIMAL(5,2)	No	0	Days carried forward from the prior window, already capped per LeavePolicy.MaxCarryForwardDays
AccruedDays	DECIMAL(5,2)	No	0	Entitlement accrued during this window, from LeavePolicy.AnnualEntitlementDays
ConsumedDays	DECIMAL(5,2)	No	0	Days actually taken — cached from the leave transaction ledger, out of scope (Section 1.2), same pattern as WarehouseItem.QuantityOnHand (Section 6.1.2)
EncashedDays	DECIMAL(5,2)	No	0	Days paid out via LeaveEncashment (Section A.7.13) against this window
DaysAvailable	DECIMAL(5,2)	No	0	Closing balance — OpeningBalance + AccruedDays − ConsumedDays − EncashedDays
Constraints: UQ_EmployeeLeaveBalance_EmployeeTypeWindow — unique (EmployeeId, LeaveTypeId, LeaveYearStartDate); CK_EmployeeLeaveBalance_Window — CHECK (LeaveYearEndDate > LeaveYearStartDate); CK_EmployeeLeaveBalance_Formula — CHECK (DaysAvailable = OpeningBalance + AccruedDays − ConsumedDays − EncashedDays).
Business rules:
•	For a CalendarYear leave type, LeaveYearStartDate/EndDate are January 1 and December 31; for a ServiceYear type, the anchor-plus-elapsed-years computation from Section A.7.10 applies, the same logic used to resolve a rotating shift roster's current cycle week (Section A.7.2).
•	When a leave-year window closes, its raw unused balance (OpeningBalance + AccruedDays − ConsumedDays) is compared against LeavePolicy.MaxCarryForwardDays for that leave type: up to the cap becomes the next window's OpeningBalance; anything beyond the cap is either encashed via LeaveEncashment (Section A.7.13), if LeavePolicy.IsEncashable = 1, or simply lapses — recorded as neither carried nor encashed, with no row created for the forfeited amount, since there is nothing further to track once a day is gone.
•	A new EmployeeLeaveBalance row is opened at the start of each leave-year window rather than the prior year's row being modified — the full history of what an employee's Earned Leave balance was, window by window, remains reconstructable indefinitely.
Sample data — Karim Hossain's Earned Leave across two consecutive service-year windows, showing the full carry-forward chain end to end, alongside the simpler Casual Leave case (which never carries forward):
Id	Employee Ref.	Leave Type Ref.	Window	Opening	Accrued	Consumed	Encashed	Available
1	Farhan Ahmed	Casual Leave	2026-01-01 to 2026-12-31	0.0	10.0	3.5	0.0	6.5
2	Farhan Ahmed	Earned Leave	2026-01-01 to 2026-12-31	0.0	18.0	6.0	0.0	12.0
3	Karim Hossain	Casual Leave	2026-01-01 to 2026-12-31	0.0	10.0	5.0	0.0	5.0
4	Karim Hossain	Earned Leave	2024-06-01 to 2025-05-31	2.0	18.0	2.0	3.0	15.0
5	Karim Hossain	Earned Leave	2025-06-01 to 2026-05-31	15.0	18.0	8.5	0.0	24.5
Rows 4 and 5 are the same leave entitlement, two windows apart, chained together: row 4's raw unused balance (2.0 + 18.0 − 2.0 = 18.0) exceeded LeavePolicy's 15-day cap by 3.0 days, which LeaveEncashment (Section A.7.13) paid out — leaving exactly 15.0 to close the window and become row 5's OpeningBalance. Row 3 shows the contrast: Casual Leave has MaxCarryForwardDays = 0, so its OpeningBalance is always 0.0 regardless of what was left unused the year before — it simply lapses, by policy, not by omission.
A.7.13 Table: LeaveEncashment
Purpose: pays an employee for unused leave days while still in service — distinct from FinalSettlementComponent's LEAVE_ENCASH line (Section A.10.4), which covers encashment at separation. A LeaveEncashment row is never created for a separation event, and a FinalSettlementComponent LEAVE_ENCASH line is never created for an in-service one; the two are mutually exclusive by which table records them, not by a status flag someone has to remember to set correctly.
Primary Key: LeaveEncashmentId (BIGINT IDENTITY)
Foreign Keys: EmployeeId → Employee; LeaveTypeId → LeaveType; EmployeeLeaveBalanceId → EmployeeLeaveBalance; ApprovedBy → UserAccount
Column	Data Type	Null	Default	Description
LeaveEncashmentId	BIGINT IDENTITY	No	auto	Surrogate primary key
EmployeeId	INT	No	—	The employee being paid
LeaveTypeId	INT	No	—	The leave type encashed
EmployeeLeaveBalanceId	BIGINT	No	—	Which closed window's excess this encashment pays out
EncashedDays	DECIMAL(5,2)	No	—	Number of days encashed
DailyRate	DECIMAL(18,2)	No	—	Rate per day the encashment is calculated at, typically the employee's Basic Salary (Section A.5.3) divided by a policy-defined days-per-month divisor
EncashedAmount	DECIMAL(18,2)	No	—	EncashedDays × DailyRate
EncashmentReason	VARCHAR(20)	No	—	CapExceeded or VoluntaryRequest
EncashmentDate	DATE	No	—	Date the payment is processed
ApprovedBy	INT	No	—	The approving manager
Constraints: CK_LeaveEncashment_Reason — CHECK (EncashmentReason IN ('CapExceeded','VoluntaryRequest')); CK_LeaveEncashment_Amount — CHECK (EncashedAmount = EncashedDays * DailyRate).
Business rules:
•	EncashmentReason = 'CapExceeded' is generated automatically when a leave-year window closes with a raw unused balance above LeavePolicy.MaxCarryForwardDays and LeavePolicy.IsEncashable = 1 (Section A.7.12's business rules); EncashmentReason = 'VoluntaryRequest' covers a policy that additionally allows an employee to opt into encashing days they would otherwise have been permitted to carry forward.
•	Writing a LeaveEncashment row and updating its referenced EmployeeLeaveBalance.EncashedDays happen in the same transaction — the CK_EmployeeLeaveBalance_Formula constraint (Section A.7.12) is what keeps the two tables from ever disagreeing about how many days were actually paid out.
•	EncashedDays can never exceed LeavePolicy.MaxEncashableDays (Section A.7.11) for the relevant leave type, employment type, and nationality category — if the amount otherwise payable is larger, only MaxEncashableDays is paid, and the remainder simply lapses rather than being carried again or queued for a later payout. The same ceiling is applied identically at final settlement (Section A.10.4), so an employee cannot receive a larger payout by leaving the company than by having it encashed during service.
Sample data — the 3.0-day cap-exceeded encashment referenced in Section A.7.12's worked example, closing out Karim Hossain's 2024–2025 Earned Leave window:
Id	Employee Ref.	Balance Window Ref.	Days	Daily Rate	Amount	Reason
1	Karim Hossain	Earned Leave, 2024-06-01–2025-05-31	3.0	1,200.00	3,600.00	CapExceeded
